                        Days of 1883

                August 28 - 29, 1932

The two day celebration began on Sunday with a ball game between
Mobridge and Java, which was won 1 to 0 by the former after nine
innings of real fast baseball.

Festivities commenced on Monday morning, with a mammoth parade,
featuring old time costumes, vehicles depicting the days when this
country was founded and other relics of by-gone days. The parade
extended over the entire length of Main street and was reported to have
been one of the finest features ever witnessed in this part of the
county. Three prizes were awarded for the best floats, the ox drawn
cart by John Haux taking first; the sod shanty of John Bauer and Wm.
Jesser, second and the paper covered shack by Arend Lakeman and J. C.
Schlepp was awarded third place.

The open air program, which took place on Main street after the parade
was opened with an address by the city mayor, Wm. Kunz. His address was
followed by short talks about their experiences in the old days by the
following pioneers: Arne Strand, G. W. Ryan, Geo. Zeller, H.P. Hall,
B. P. Adams and John Erbe. The misses Nettie Kunz, Freda Jesser, Vivian
Bauer and Mrs. A. W. Spiry sang several songs in costume and another
fine musical number was the Miss Vivian Bauer's solo. The comic number
by six minstrels included: Ed, Wilfred and Ervin Kunz, Henry Hauck, Levi
Krause and Arthur Buckenberger and was hugely enjoyed by the crowd.

Music during the day was furnished by the "Hungry German Five" in
costume, consisting of I. R. Krause, Sam E. Spiry, J. C. Schlepp,
Rueben Schlaht and A. C. Schlepp.

The rodeo was an outstanding event of the day with close to 2,000
people attending the event. The McMacken brothers of Trail City, a
group of riders from Lowry and several outside riders giving a good
exhibition of their skill in the art of riding and bull dogging.

Governor Warren E. Green and Joe Parmley spoke before a large gathering
after the rodeo.

Various street sports and dancing in the evening rounded out one of the
biggest days ever held in Java. The Art B. Thomas Carnival shows were
on the midway both days.

        John Haux drove the team with Governor Green and Joe Parmley as
        riders in the parade in the 1883 Celebration.

        Costumes were the order of the day as Java held the Days of
        1883 Celebration, which included a big Minstrel show.

                        Early Java Organizations
                Junior Red Cross

On September 20, 1917, a Junior Red Cross was organized in Java. Tillie
Dufloth and Helen Aman were the organizers. Members were Helen Aman,
chairman; Tillie Dufloth, secretary; and Lorraine Schott, treasurer.
Other members were Gretchen Clement, Leota Schott, Elizabeth Noble,
Agnes and Elizabeth Gehrki.

                Girl Hiker's Club

The Girl Hikers' club made their first hike on Tuesday, September 27,
1921. Their destination was Haux's Grove. They gathered wood and soon
had a fire blazing. Supper was spread, roasted wieners forming a large
part of the menu. Jokes were laughingly exchanged and snatches of songs
could be heard. Occasionally a picture was snapped when the opportunity
presented itself. At dusk a meeting was held for the purpose of
electing officers, who were: Chairman, Rozella Hunter, and treasurer,
Pearl Forbes. Other members were: Gretta Finch, Grethel Finch,
Genevieve Fargen, Rose Rissky, Freya Clement and Eunice Campbell. The
plan was to increase the hike two miles each trip in order to gradually
become accustomed to the strain.

                The Java Literary Society

During February of 1906, a large number of Java citizens met at the
schoolhouse for the purpose of organizing a Literary Society. Kurt
Eisentraut presided and C. F. Clement was selected as secretary. The
organization was effected and different committees appointed. A debate
was held upon the question "Resolved that ancient times are greater
than modern times." E. E. Reber, L. O'Reilly and E. W. Sackett took the
affirmative; A. I. O'Reilly, J. Stratton and Art McCafferty the
negative. H. J. Cook, Mrs. H. L. Hughes and C. F. Clement were selected
as judges, the decision being negative.

On March 3, 1906 another meeting was held to adopt a constitution and
bylaws, allowing every person with good moral character, regardless of
age to become a member. Each became a member upon paying a membership
fee of ten cents, and monthly dues of the amount of ten cents.
Sixty-five members enrolled at this time. A permanent organization was
effected by the election of the following officers: President C. F.
Clement; vice president, Arend Lakeman, secretary, L. Pratton and
treasurer, Mabel Smith. At the March 17th meeting, sixteen more members
were added, bringing the total membership to 81.

                LADIES AID SOCIETY

The women of Java and vicinity held a meeting at the home of Mrs.
Gerrit Noble on July 14, 1904, for the purpose of organizing a Ladies
Aid Society. The following ladies were elected as officers: President,
Laura E. Harris; vice president, Mrs. James Corson; secretary, Mrs.
Philip Noble; treasurer, Mrs. Jacob Stoller. Their meetings were held
every two weeks, the members alternating in hosting the bi-weekly
meetings.

                KNIGHTS OF THE MACCABEES

C. K. Hair of Aberdeen, the state commander of K.O.T.M assisted by J.
V. Arnold and L. E. Smith of Ipswich, Dr. Beil S. J. Hoffman, J. D.
Stewart, John Bibelheimer, Clarence and Ben Clark of Selby, instituted
a new tent of that order in Java on the night of February 16, 1904. The
following officers were installed: Post Commander, G. P. Vick; Commander,
C. F. Clement, Lt. Commander, Henry Thielen; Recordkeeper, W. L.
McCafferty, Finance-Keeper, Jacob Stoller; Chaplain, John Hofmeyer,
Physician, Sigmund Rosenthal-Sergeant, C. Huether; Master-at-Arms,
Joseph Bosch; 1st Master of Guards, Kurt Eisentraut; 2nd Master of
Guards, C. C. Gross: Sentinel, Daniel Reide; Picket, J. Eberhart.

                LADY MACCABEES

In March of 1910, Mrs. Hattie L. Westmeier of Bath, came to Java to
organize a local lodge of the Lady Maccabees. She got twenty of the
best ladies in town to join. They were examined by Dr. Beil of Selby,
and all passed examinations successfully.

The ladies were: Mrs. Emily Hicks, Mrs. Tina Gehrki, Mrs. Ellen
Hofmeyer, Mrs. Johanna Schott, Mrs. Maria Buckenberger, Mrs. Magdalena
Hepper, Mrs. Mary Anderson, Mrs. Julia Borchert, Mrs. Emma Click, Mrs.
Grace Lehmann, Mrs. Kate Clement, Mrs. Beatrice Chittick, Mrs. Bertha
Schlepp, Mrs. Ella Taylor, Miss Myrtle Roberts, Miss Mary Beckman, Miss
Lillian Heerwald, Miss Ethel Hicks, Miss Cora Stuart, Miss Ethel Ryan,
Miss Nora Schott and Miss Helen Ryan. Mrs. S. N. Hereford of Aberdeen,
Lady State Commander, installed the following officers: Post Commander,
Mrs. Grace Lehmann; Commander, Mrs. Johanne Schott; Lt. Commander, Mrs.
Tina Gehrki; Record Keeper, Mrs. Emily Hicks; Finance Auditor, Mrs. Kate
Clement; Chaplain, Mrs. Julia Borchert; Sergeant, Miss Nora Schott;
Mistress-at-Arms, Miss Mary Beckman; Sentinel, Mrs. Ella Taylor;
Picket, Mrs. Ellen Hofmeyer; Capt. of the Guards, Miss Myrtle Roberts;
Platoon No. 1, Miss Ethel Hicks; Platoon No. 2, Miss Lillian Heerwald;
Pianist, Mrs. Bertha Schlepp.

                CHATAUQUA READING CIRCLE

Sometime during September, 1911 a number of ladies organized a
Chatauqua Reading Circle: Mrs. R. M. Hennings, president; Mrs. E. B.
Adams, vice president; and Miss Bernice Gordon, secretary.

                CAMP FIRE GIRLS SOCIETY

In November of 1913, the girls of the Java high school organized a Camp
Fire Girls Society. This organization was under the leadership of Mrs.
Fred Bauer.

                JAVA EASTERN STAR CHAPTER NO. 123

The Java Chapter of the Eastern Star was organized on July 24, 1916,
the Mobridge Chapter helping institute the Chapter. The ceremonies took
place in the Masonic Lodge rooms of Java, which were tastefully
decorated with flowers and greens by the local Star members. Worthy
Grand Patron Dr. B. Hart of Blunt, assisted by Mrs. Hart, held the
meeting. Otto Doherr of Selby, acted as Grand Secretary, reading and
presenting the Charter. Miss Rose Schmidt of Selby, acted as Worthy
Matron and Mrs. A. Beil of Selby, as Grand Marshal.

Officers elected were: Worthy Matron, Johanna Schott; Worthy Patron,
Jacob J. Wolff; Associate Matron, Sarah B. Hughes; Secretary, Grace
Noble; Treasurer, W. C. Hicks; Conductress, Pearl Hennings; Associate
Conductress, Emily T. Hicks; Chaplain, Clara Barclay; Marshal, Richard
Bellis; Organist, Vina Varnum. Star Points: Ada, Alice Noble; Ruth,
Louise Bohle; Esther, Emilie Eisentraut Martha, Kate Clement; and
Electa, Carrie Noteboom. Warden, Emma Wolff; Sentinel, H. J. Cook.

In December of 1938, Java Chapter surrendered its Charter and
consolidated with the Hope Chapter of Selby. Seventeen members coming
into the Hope Chapter at this time.

                MASONIC LODGE

Local Masons tried to organize a lodge at Java as far back as 1908.
Several previous attempts to organize a lodge were made, but always
without success. In April of 1913, the local Masons rented the Helm
Hall and had it remodeled. In November of 1914, the Java Masonic Lodge
was formally instituted with Dr. Sigmond Rosenthal installed as
Worshipful Master. Other officers installed were Philip Noble, E. H.
Noteboom, Jacob Helm, H. J. Cook, W. E. Cole, Fred Krause, I. C.
Pratton, H. A. Taylor and H. L. Hughes.

                JAVA COMMERCIAL LEAGUE

The businessmen of Java, held a meeting on the night of April 18, 1905
for the purpose of organizing a businessmen's club. Every business man
became a member except one. On Thursday night of April 20, 1905 a board
of directors were chosen, which were John Hofmeyer, W. C. Hicks, Fred
Krause, Henry Thielen and Christ Wolf. On April 29, another meeting was
held at the C. F. Clement home and the following officers were elected:
President, Kurt Eisentraut; 1st Vice President, H. J. Cook; 2nd Vice
President, Jacob Stoller, Recording Secretary, Christ Huether;
Corresponding Secretary, C. F. Clement; Treasurer, S. Rosenthal. The
club had a membership of 52 members, with meetings held the first
Tuesday of every month.

                EMBROIDERY CLUB

A new club was organized at the "Dove Cote" on November 23, 1922. The
club was christened "Embroidery Club" and each lady member came with
her fancy work. The club members were Mrs. W. C. Hicks, Mrs. G. W.
Hicks, Mrs. E. C. Giffen, Mrs. R. M. Hennings, Miss Pearl Forbes,
Rozella Hunter, Rita Ryan, Freya Clement and Genevieve Fargen.

                GRANDMOTHERS CLUB

In October, 1934, the Birthday Club met with Mrs. Mae Campbell and Mrs.
H. J. Cook as hostesses at the Cook home Sunday at one o'clock dinner.
It was decided to organize a club to be known as Grandmother's Club.
Meetings were to be held every third Saturday afternoon of the month and
lunches to be served.

The following officers of the club were elected: Mrs. Kate Clement,
chairman; Mrs. Gerrit Noble, vice chairman, Mrs. H. J. Cook, secretary.
Those present were Mrs. B. S. Stowell of Selby, Mesdames: Peter Noble,
W. C. Hicks, Philip Noble, Kate Clement and the hostesses.

                        Java Organizations
                JAVA GARDEN CLUB

In the early summer of 1953, the Home Arts Extension club was
instrumental in starting the Java Garden Club. At that time a meeting
was held at the home of Letha Bieber. Three ladies from Mobridge came
to help organize the club. They were Mrs. Bastian, Mrs. Briley, and
Mrs. Davidson. Not all the members of the Home Arts Extension club
became members of the Garden Club. Among the original members were:
Letha Bieber, Lila Bieber, Nettie Kast, Rose Jacobs, Emma Fiedler, Tina
Gehrki, Marie Hicks, Mary Hirsch, Rosie Spiry, Emma Stiegelmeier,
Maggie Vaagen, Martha Wolff, Lois Wolf, Lizzie Zeller, Hazel Zeller, Eva
Bauer and Janice Wolf.

The new Java Garden Club was organized just two days before the
Mobridge flower show. At that time Mobridge held large shows at the
auditorium. Several members of the newly formed club took a total of
over eighty entries to the Mobridge show and won a fair share of
ribbons.

Soon after this original meeting, Mrs. Dale Summers of the Women's
Fellowship of the Aberdeen Plymouth Congregational Church was contacted
to secure a copy of the Constitution and By-Laws of their garden club
and this document was used for their Constitution and By-Laws. Later
the date for the election of officers was changed from July to January,
at the request of the state president. In the beginning the club was
federated, but in January of '58 it withdrew temporarily. Attempts were
made in '59, '67 and '69 to again federate but the issue was always
defeated by one vote except once when the motion was withdrawn.

As early as March in 1956 the club purchased a picnic table for the
City Park. The club has also taken part in various community activities.

In 1960 and again in 1961 they had a float in the Homecoming Parade. In
1968-1969 and 1970, with the Commercial Club, they sponsored and helped
judge Christmas Lighting Contests. Also in 1969 the club took a very
active part in the All Classes Alumni Reunion. At that time with the
extension clubs they conducted a very successful hobby show. Lunches
were served.

        Grandmothers Club -- (All Pioneers) Left to right: Mrs. Philip
        Noble, Mrs. H. J. Cook, Mrs. Peter Noble, Mrs. Gerrit Noble, Mrs.
        Bradley Stowell, Mrs. Kate Clement, Mrs. Emily Hicks.

It is believed that their first annual flower show was held in 1956.
Since then 8 shows have been held in June and 10 in July or August. The
club is proud of their shows and Silver Teas as they were well
patronized. The club has in its permanent possession, a cup awarded by
the Mobridge club for having the most blue ribbons at their show 3
years in succession.

Since 1967 the club has created a beauty spot on Main street on the lot
north of the Legion Hall. In 1972 the club secured title to the lot
with the help of Henry H. Wolff when it was sold for taxes.

Every summer there is a continuous display of bloom, beginning with
tulips followed in season by iris, peonies, day lilies and roses. Every
year a large circular bed of annuals is planted, usually petunias and
alyssum. The members take turns caring for our garden plot. Three trees
have also been planted, a Mountain Ash and 2 Blue Spruce. A white fence
makes an attractive background.

The Garden Club year follows almost a fixed pattern: Tour of
greenhouses in May; flower show and tea in June, July or August; a
family picnic, usually in July; a tour of members' gardens when they
are at their best, slides shown in October and a Christmas party in
early December or late November. The club has participated in
benevolences of various kinds.

Almost from the beginning the members have enjoyed having and being
Secret Sisters. They observe anniversaries, birthdays, and Christmas,
and any other occasions they wish. Their identities are revealed at the
Christmas party and new names drawn for the coming year.

For the past few years each Java Garden Club member has given a
Christmas gift for a resident of the Good Samaritan Center in Selby. On
March 23, 1974 the Garden Club held a baked food sale, at which lunch
was also sold in addition to craft and hobby items, which were donated
by the members. The proceeds were given to be used in the Diamond
Jubilee Fund. October 24, the club presented a program of slides of
Java gardens and homes to the residents of the Good Samaritan Center,
after which they served cookies for all the residents.

Lorraine Fiechtner, Viola Goetz, Violet Goetz, Marie Hicks, Maggie
Kary, Nettie Kast, Molly Mueller, Francis Naasz, Tillie Schnaible, Alma
Sonnenfeld, Hilda Stangl, Martha Wolff, Hazel Zeller.

                SEW AND SO EXTENSION CLUB

The Sew and So extension club was organized Nov. 12, 1948 by Mrs. Jake
Rabenberg and Mrs. Merl Masteller of Selby, at the old Java Legion
Hall. Twelve members joined this club at this time. They were Mmes.
Fred C. Bieber, Herbert Bieber, Gerhardt Fiedler, Lee Bell, Joe
Mueller, John Woodruff, Art Eisenbeisz, Gilbert Schlepp, Rueben
Schmierer, Christ Pfitzer, and Alvin Spiry; and Miss Elizabeth Scott
(Mrs. Otto Schnaible). The first officers were: President, Mrs. Reuben
Schmierer; vice president, Mrs. Alvin Spiry and secretary-treasurer,
Mrs. Christ Pfitzer.

During the year 1949-50, seven new members joined the club. The first
annual picnic was held at Lake Hiddenwood. The club also sponsored a
4-H club for a number of years. It was called the Nimble Fingers 4-H
club. A craft project started by the club in 1951-52 was textile
painting. A glow candle demonstration was enjoyed by the club members in
1952-53. During the year 1955, they started a cookie and candy exchange
at Christmas, which they still do, and also started entertaining their
husbands with an annual party. In the year 1956-57, the club entered
exhibits at the Mobridge Northern Show of Progress and received a
plaque for the most entries. The club, along with the Home Arts Club,
participated in the March of Dimes drive in which a cake was raffled
off. In the same year a set of by-laws for the club was drawn up and
adopted.

In 1958, the club was hostess for the Walworth County Spring Tea, which
was held at the Java School. In 1959, they received permanent
possession of the plaque from the Northern Show of Progress at
Mobridge. The club had won it for the past three years for having the
most exhibits. In May, 1959 many club members attended the Extension
Club's District meeting held at Pollock and were treated to a tour of
the new town of Pollock. In 1966, the club won 2nd place on a booth
entitled "Plaster Plus" at the Walworth County Christmas Fair, for
which they were given a ribbon. In 1967, the Sew & So club, along with
the Home Arts club hosted the Walworth County Spring Tea at the Java
Legion Hall. In June, 1968, at a club meeting eleven members had quite
an experience as they were forced to take shelter in a root cellar
while a tornado roared around them. However every-one survived and the
home received little damage from the storm. In 1970, one of the members
received state-wide attention when she was named Farm Wife of the week
by the Aberdeen American News. She was Mrs. Herbert Fiechtner. In April
of the same year, the International Festival and Tasting Tea was held
at the Java school auditorium. The club were hostesses again in 1972
when the Hobby Holiday was held at the Java school. At the Walworth
County Spring Tea held at Mobridge in 1974, four members received their
25 year pins. These charter members are: Mrs. Fred C. Bieber, Mrs.
Gerhardt Fiedler, Mrs. Otto Schnaible, and Mrs. Alvin Spiry. Two other
charter members: Mrs. Joe Mueller and Mrs. Herbert Bieber are deceased.
Present members of the Sew & So extension club are: Mmes. Herbert
Fiechtner, Fred C. Bieber, Joel Fiechtner, Gerhardt Fiedler, Ervin
Mueller, Harold Pfitzer, Marvin Pfitzer, Robert Pfitzer, LaRayne
Schlepp, Cornelius Schnaible, Otto Schnaible, Walt Simons, Erwin
Sonnenfeld, Alvin Spiry, Dave Witlock and John Witlock.

Besides their regular demonstrations over the years, they have
participated in many different types of hobbies which have enriched
their lives.

                JAVA PTO

The Java unit of the Parent-Teacher Association was organized October
5,1955, in the high school auditorium by a group of parents, teacher
and clergy. The purpose of the association is educational and is
devoted to child welfare. It endeavors to create a better understanding
between parents and public schools.

The meeting was called to order under the supervision of Mrs. Norman
Anderson of Lowry, the District 11 P.T.A. president, sister-in-law of
Eddie Anderson of Java. She explained the purpose of the meeting and
discussed the work of the Parent-Teacher Association. The following
officers were elected: President, Mrs. Carl Jacobs; vice president,
Eddie Anderson; secretary, Mrs. Emory Darling; treasurer, Mrs. Ervin
Wolf. The second Monday of each month was selected as the night for
P.T.A. to meet. The meetings opened with group singing, reading of the
Objects of the P.T.A. and the Pledge to the Flag. This was followed by
the business meeting, various conventions, Founders Day and Workshop
reports.

During the past years many fine programs have been presented by the
students, teachers, parents and out-of-town guests. Since its beginning
many projects have been undertaken to provide funds to purchase such
items as: photocopier, basketball uniforms, film projector, library
books, tape recorder and public address system, to name just a few.

The annual P.T.A. Convention for District 11 was held in the Java
school auditorium in October of 1963; also, the spring P.T.A.
Conference in April of 1968.

In October of 1970 the Association voted to leave the state and
national P.T.A. and become the local Parent-Teacher Organization. Other
changes have also taken place. The fourth Monday of the months of
September, November, January, and March are presently the evenings when
the P.T.O. meets.

The present officers are: President, Mrs. Paul Jacobs; vice president,
Mrs. Carlyle Bieber; secretary, Mrs. Merlyn Melcher; treasurer, Mrs.
Donald Sauer. Time and space does not permit us to mention all who
served on committees and as officers but we wish to pay tribute to them
for their years of faithful service. The history of the P.T.O. is not
closed. It is impossible to predict the future, but it is encouraging
to find that almost all who have been members of this organization have
come away with a deeper understanding and appreciation of the work that 
goes on between parents and teachers for the students.

                JAVA BANDMOTHERS

This organization was started on February 28, 1961. During the first
year the Java Band Mothers Club was a busy group. The forty members
donated food and time, and with the wonderful support and cooperation
of all the people in the community, they were able to make a generous
donation toward the purchase of 40 new uniforms for the Java school
band.

Mrs. Ed Anderson was elected the club's first president; Mrs. Floyd
Biel, vice president; and Mrs. Otto Mueller, secretary-treasurer.
Gerald Cutshaw was advisor of the club.

The club has had various means of raising funds, such as sponsoring
carnivals, serving meals, food sales, a town vs. country basketball
game, postoffice auction, card parties and selling community calendars.
They have sponsored two to three students each year to attend
International Music Camp at the Peace Gardens for many years. Various
new instruments have been purchased through the years.

At present there are thirty-nine band mothers.

        Java Chamber of Commerce Dinner, around 1949.

                CHAMBER OF COMMERCE

Minutes of the Java Chamber of Commerce, the official organization of
the City of Java, were recorded from the years 1947-1958. On Jan. 20,
1947, a meeting was held for the purpose of organizing a Chamber of
Commerce. Temporary chairman at this time was J. S. Pfitzer. Literature
from the various Chambers of Commerce were presented by A. C. Schlepp,
and the benefits of such an organization was discussed. Everyone
present was in favor of organizing an official commercial organization
such as a Chamber of Commerce. It was suggested that a committee of
five be appointed (together with the temporary chairman) to draw up a
set of bylaws and a rate schedule for the different classes of
membership. Serving on this committee, in addition to Mr. Pfitzer, were
the following: G. W. Hicks, Frank Weisgram, Reinhold Moser, Ben
Stiegelmeier, and Leon Kast. The minutes of this first meeting were
signed by A. C. Schlepp, temporary secretary.

On March 11, 1947, a special meeting was held to elect officers for the
newly-formed Chamber of Commerce. These officers were as follows:
President, Chris Pfitzer; vice president, J. S. Pfitzer; secretary, A.
C. Schlepp; and treasurer, Arthur Eisenbeisz. It was moved to adopt the
third Monday of each month as the regular meeting night for the new
Chamber of Commerce.

Members of the first Chamber of Commerce were the following businesses:
Class A: Eisenbeisz and Son and the Java Equity Exchange; Class B:
Bauer and Sons, C. L. Hicks Exchange, Elmer's Market, Gill Hardware,
Job Cafe, Kary Store, Java Herald, Schlepp Drug Store, Grand Theatre,
Sterling Oil Company, Central Lumber Company, McCabe Elevator, and
Bagley Elevator; Class C: Spiry Garage, Pfitzer Garage, Schuh Repair
Shop and Weisgram Blacksmith; Class D: Individual members to include
carpenters, clerks, postmaster, mail carriers, railroad men, teachers,
farmers and laborers.

Officers of the Java Chamber of Commerce, following the first year,
were as follows: 1948: President, E. R. Kary; vice president, Leon
Kast; secretary, J. S. Pfitzer; treasurer, Arthur Eisenbeisz. 1949:
President, A. C. Schlepp; vice president, Elmer Biel; secretary, Ben

treasurer, Othmer (Ole) Naasz. 1951: President, J. S. Pfitzer; vice

Naasz. . 1952: President, J. S. Pfitzer; vice president, A. C. Schlepp;
secretary, C. J. Pfitzer; treasurer, Ole Naasz. 1953: President, J. S.
Pfitzer; vice president, Chris J. Pfitzer; secretary, Gottlieb Zeller;
treasurer, Reuben Schmierer. 1954: President, A. C. Schlepp; vice

Fiedler; 1955: the same as for 1954. 1956: President, Elmer Biel; vice

The main purpose of the Auxiliary is to help care for veterans and
their families, especially those hospitalized. Christmas gifts and
money are sent to the Hot Springs Veterans hospital, also all monthly
showers called for by the state department are donated. Gift Shop
articles, which are made by hospitalized veterans, are ordered each
year and sold locally. Poppies are made by disabled veterans and every
year the Auxiliary sponsors a poppy drive.

On Memorial Day the graves of deceased members of the Legion and
Auxiliary are decorated with flags and wreaths. After the Memorial Day
program the Auxiliary has served a dinner at the Legion Hall. Other
means of raising funds are card parties, and serving lunches at dances
and turkey raffles.

The Auxiliary has sponsored a Girls Stater every year beginning in
1949. A Poppy Poster contest and an Americanism Essay contest is held
each year and cash prizes are given to the winners.

The Auxiliary is proud to have Mrs. Lydia Bauer as a Gold Star Mother.

In 1974 new officers were elected as follows: President, Mrs. Marvin
Pfitzer; 1st vice president, Mrs. Gus Schaffer 2nd vice president, Mrs.
Jake Nehlich; secretary, Mrs. Harold Pfitzer, treasurer, Mrs. Gerhardt
Fiedler, historian Mrs. Wilbur Krause; sergeant at arms, Mrs. Alex
Krause, chaplain, Mrs. Leonard Schmidtgall. The Auxiliary consists of
twenty-two members and six junior members. The following eight charter
members remain on the roll at this writing: Mrs. Gerhardt Fiedler, Mrs.
Emanuel Schlepp, Mrs. Jake Nehlich, Mrs. William Frank, Mrs. Alex
Krause, Mrs. Adolph Pfaff, Mrs. Harold Pfitzer and Mrs. Ernest
Zimmerman (Gloria Schlepp).

        The first Java American Legion Hall

        Present Java American Legion Hall, completed in year 1961.

                JAVA AMERICAN LEGION

The Java Legion Post was organized on November 18, 1946 at a meeting
held in the old City Hall building with 35 or 40 veterans in
attendance. It was decided to name the post the LaVerne Bauer Post, No.
275 of the American Legion in honor of Flight Officer L. V. Bauer, only
son of Mr. and Mrs. John Bauer, Jr., who died in a plane crash over
China in October of 1944, and who is buried in Golden Gate National
Cemetery at San Francisco.

Anton Mueller was named as the first Commander and other officers
elected at this organizational meeting were: Gerhardt Fiedler, vice
commander; Gilbert Schlepp, adjutant; Wes Frank, sergeant at arms; G.
W. Hicks, finance officer; Othmer Naasz, historian and Elmer Biel,
chaplain. A group of businessmen had purchased the old City Hall
building sometime before and turned it over to the new Post to be used
as a Legion Hall. Several years later, this building was sold to Fred
Permann Plumbing and Heating.

Late in September of 1949, ground was broken for a basement for a new
Legion Hall on a lot just south of the former Grand Theatre building.
Due to lack of finances, it was decided to finish the basement and use
it as a basement hall until such time a structure could be built above
it. This dream became a reality when the Grand Theatre building was
purchased and lumber from it was used for the new hall. This was
completed by September of 1961 and a Grand Opening dance was held on
Sept. 8 with music furnished by Mike Dosch of Bismarck, and his
electric organ and Gerhardt Bauman of Hosmer, and his accordion.

Today the Post has fifty-five members, with Marvin Wolf serving as
Legion Commander.

Commanders of the Legion during the past 28 years were: Anton Mueller.
1 year; Joe Zarka, 18 years; Emanuel Schlepp, 1 year; Gilbert Schlepp,
1 year; Calvin Aman, 1 year; Gust Schaffer, 4 years; and Marvin Wolf, 1
year. Charter members were: Alex Krause, Herbert Schneider, Raymond
Hochstetter, Leonard Boehler, Arthur C. Frank, Theophil Oster, Vernard
Schneider, Clifford Naasz, Anton Mueller, Floyd Brown, Julius Wolf,
Robert Goetz, Victor Hirsch, Harold Schlepp, Ernest Zimmerman, Edward
Goetz +, Herbert Geigle, Ruben Neumiller, Raymond Goetz, Chris J.
Pfitzer, Ed Schnaible, Raymond Pfaff, Wesley Frank, Othmer Naasz,
George W. Hicks +, Fred Mittleider, Ervin Munsch+, Emil Neumiller,
William Frank+, Harold Horst, Rinel Schneider, Gerhardt Fiedler, Allen
Perman+, Fred Job+, Gilbert Schlepp+, Elmer O. Biel, Emil Boehler, Ed.
J. Schlepp, Fred Pregitzer+. + denotes deceased members.

                PRESBYTERIAN LADIES AID

The Ladies Aid Society was organized in 1904 and raised funds to help
furnish the new church. One such fund raising was the church supper.
When the Ladies had their regular meeting at the church basement on
Feb. 1, 1933, a supper was served at 5:30 p.m., with the following
menu: Roast pork, applesauce, mashed potatoes and gravy, corn, pickles,
buttered buns, pie and coffee. The ladies who were serving at this time
were: Mmes. W. C. Hicks, J. C. Schlepp, Kate Clement, Carl Jacobs, G.
W. Hicks and Otto B. Wolff. The usual prices of 20 cents for adults and
10 cents for children under 12 years was charged.

They also purchased the bell, which was later destroyed by the fire of
1936. They have assisted the church financially throughout the years.
Among the early members were: Mmes. Kate Clement, W. C. Hicks, H. J.
Cook, Gerrit Noble, Peter Noble, W. F. Dyer, Winkley, Eisentraut,
Fiechtner, L. E. Harris, J. Stoller, W. F. Ewald and H. Theilen.

                        ALCW

The American Lutheran Church Women of St. Paul Lutheran church of Java,
which at first was known as the Ladies Aid Society, was organized in
the summer of 1905, under the leadership of Pastor George Kirchdoerfer
at the Lutheran parsonage, by the following four ladies, Mrs. George
Kirchdoerfer, Mrs. Christ Schnaible, Mrs. Christ Wolf and Mrs. Jacob
Fiechtner. Having no public meeting place, the Aid met in private homes
for many years, until the basement under the church was completed.
Through the years, many fund-raising projects were carried out by this
organization, the proceeds of which were contributed to the many
charities of the church, besides helping financially with improvements
of the church interior.

In 1946, the first edition of the Aid's cookbook was published,
containing recipes brought by the members. On Feb. 1, 1951, the
pastor's wife, Mrs. E. H. Haacke, introduced the work of the Women's
Missionary Federation and at that meeting, the Ladies Aid voted to
become affiliated with the W.M.F. and the English language was used at
the meetings. Results of that bonded effort were very gratifying,
supporting the eight departments of the W.M.F., besides assisting with
the needs of the local church and congregation. At that time there were
63 members.

When the new church became a reality, the W.M.F. held their first
meeting in the new church on May 10, 1960. Besides donating a
substantial sum to the building fund, the Society fully equipped the
new kitchen and also purchased four sets of paraments. It had a
membership of 110 members of which one charter members, Mrs. Christ
Wolf, was still with the organization .

In December 1960, the name of the Society was changed again,--this time
to the American Lutheran church Women, as the auxiliary of the American
Lutheran Church adopted this name. It has remained as such up to the
present time.

In 1961, the members were divided into five working groups, each group
taking its turn in working for church functions and funerals.

It was decided in 1963 to publish a second edition of the A.L.C.W.
cookbook, with recipes added by members of the present Society. Two
thousand cook books were published and sold.

Bible study groups were started in January, 1965. There are 5 groups at
the present time, four meeting in their classrooms, with a different
group each month remaining in the social room with the honorary
members.

As of now, there are 171 members in this organization, which meets as a
group the first Wednesday evening of each month. This group continues
to contribute much to the congregation and various charities and
Missions. The last charter member, Mrs. Christ (Rosina) Wolf passed away
Dec. 28, 1972 at the age of 87.

Present officers of the organization are: President, Mrs. Ervin
Mueller; vice president, Mrs. John Witlock; secretary, Mrs. Erven Aman,
treasurer, Mrs. Fred C. Bieber; education secretary, Mrs. Wilbur
Krause; stewardship secretary, Mrs. Merle Noess.

Honorary members are: Mmes. Eddie Anderson, Lydia Bauer, Fred Bauman,
Henry Bender, William Frank, Ed Gill, John Goetz, Ed Goetz, Jacob J.
Haux, Margaretha Haux, Jacob Himmerich, Marie Hofer, Adolph C. Horst,
John Kapfenstein, Emanuel Keller, I. C. Krause, Rudolph Kurle, David
Lemley, Jake Nehlich, Ed Naasz, Margaret Naasz, Eva Ochszner, Christina
Oster, Fred Permann, Lydia Pfaff, Julius Pfitzer, Linda Pregitzer,
Reinhold Sackman, Frieda Sauer, William Sauer, John Schadler, David
Schlepp, Emanuel Schlepp, Michael Schmierer, Ed Schnaible, Henry
Schreter, Adolph Schuh, C. F. (Rosie) Spiry, Jacob Sonnenfeld, Philip
F. (Theresa) Spiry, Philip H. (Natalie) Spiry, Sam Spiry, Adolph
Stangl, Ben Stiegelmeier, Jacob Teske, Frank Weisgram, Carrie Wolf,
Edward Wolf, William (Bill) Wolf, Henry H. Wolff, and Gottlieb (Carrie)
Zeller.

                ALTAR GUILD

A group of women were organized in January of 1960 under the leadership
of Pastor Arthur Schmitt. This became the Altar Guild of St. Paul
Lutheran Church. The organization meets on the second Monday of the
month.

The Altar Guild not only takes care of the chancel and furnishings,
altar paraments, brass ware and preparing for communion, but also
arranges the flowers used on the altar. Of the 12 charter members the
following are still members: Mrs. Henry Bender, Mrs. Fred C. Bieber,
Mrs. Harold Fiechtner, Mrs. Bob Goetz, Mrs. Julius Stangl, Mrs. Amelia
Wolf, Mrs. Henry H. Wolff, Mrs. John Zeller, Mrs. Henry Mueller Jr. and
Mrs. Lydia Bauer. Mrs. Christ Naasz and Mrs. Herbert Bieber (deceased)
were also charter members. The following ladies are also presently
members: Mrs. Jake Nehlich, Mrs. Lydia Frank, Mrs. Lydia Pfaff, Mrs.
Herbert Fiechtner, Mrs. Elmer Schmierer, Mrs. Herman Fiedler, Mrs.
Reinhold Sackman, Mrs. Dave Schlepp, Mrs. Ervin Sonnenfeld, Mrs.
Emanuel Keller, Mrs. Oscar Schnaible, Mrs. Merle Noess, Mrs. Otto
Mueller, Mrs. Otto Schnaible and Mrs. Julius Wolf.

The first officers were: President, Mrs. Arthur Schmitt; vice
president, Mrs. John Zeller and secretary-treasurer, Mrs. Henry H.
Wolff.

The Altar Guild purchases for the church have always been financed by
free will offerings by the members at each meeting .

Over the years the Altar Guild has made and embroidered fair linen and
baptismal napkins, pacific cloth communion ware bags and Christmas tree
skirts. They also made the chrismons, purchased a pall, usher badges,
anchor bar for altar, pottery vases, refrigerator for flowers, miniature
Christmas lites and guest book.

They plant and care for the evergreens and each year purchase and plant
flowers. All the candles for the advent wreath and those used for
decorating the windows during the Christmas season are supplied by the
Altar Guild.

In 1970 the first annual guest night was held. On guest night a special
program is given and usually results in adding new members to the
organization. Beginning in 1971 a hospitality chairman acknowledges the
visitors to our church by sending out cards.

One afternoon in December is set aside to mend and press the choir
robes and usually on the same day the candles, greens and bows are put
on the windows.

Through the program our pastor presents, we have learned much about the
Bible, the symbols, paraments and various phases of living a Christian
life. Our purpose is always to beautify the service and keep all things
in order. We look ahead and plan for specific needs throughout the year.

                HOME ARTS CLUB

The Home Arts Extension Club was organized in the fall of 1948 through
the efforts of Mrs. Martin Gehrki, who was assisted by Mrs. Jake
Rabenberg and County Agent, Robert Fahlberg.

The club name was submitted by Mrs. Albert Bender, now deceased. Of the
14 original members, only one, Mrs. Herman Fiedler, has remained
continuously active in the organization. However, five members have
previously received their 25 year pins as they had been extension club
members before this club was organized. They are: Mrs. Emily Hicks
(deceased), Mrs. Martin Gehrki, Mrs. F. E. Bauer, Mrs. Rosie Spiry and
Mrs. Eddie Anderson. In 1973, a plant was presented to Mrs. Gehrki, at
the Americana Nursing Center at Aberdeen, as a tribute to her for the
25th year the club had been in existence.

Five members have been elected to county offices: Mrs. Martin Gehrki,
Mrs. Herman Fiedler, Mrs. Art F. Bieber and Mrs. Gus Schaffer have
served as treasurer; Mrs. Herman Fiedler has served as secretary and
Mrs. Ronald Fiedler is now serving as vice president and has also
served as county leader for the Personal Enrichment project.

Throughout the years of the club's existence they have taken part in
many community activities. Donations of food, clothing and household
items were given to several needy families, who had lost their home in
fires. Cash donations have also been given to many charitable causes.
The club has also planted and maintained a flower plot in the city park
as a community betterment project. A picnic table was purchased and
donated for the city park.

In 1950, the club entered a float in the Java Golden Jubilee parade,
for which they were awarded a prize. In 1974, they sponsored a card
party to donate to the Diamond Jubilee fund.

Each year, gifts are presented to the State Hospital at Yankton and
also to the residents at the Rest Homes for Christmas. Once a year, the
husbands are entertained to a potluck dinner and during the summer,
they have a family picnic.

There have been some anxious moments at some meetings, too. In June of
1968, the club met at the Marvin Wolf home when a tornado struck. The
lights went out, the door flew open and the water and insulation came
pouring into the room. Although everyone was badly frightened, no one
was hurt, and all returned home safely after the storm had abated. The
next day it was discovered that the windcharger had crashed through the
roof.

The club members have consistently taken part in state and county
projects, and many were presented certificates for successfully
completing the projects. The members have gained much useful
information from lessons presented in the art of homemaking, safety,
health, cultural arts and other topics.

                JAVA VOLUNTEER FIRE DEPARTMENT

The Java Fire Department was organized on March 1, 1905 with 6 members
on Hose Co. 1 and 3 members on Engine Co. No. 1. On March 6, 1916 the
Hook and Ladder Co. 1 was used and 4 new members were added to the
department. In June of 1922, the hand drawn Chemical Engine No. 1 was
used, adding 4 more new firemen.

The Department was re-organized in November of 1939, at this time 10
new members were added to the department. In December of 1939, the
fighting equipment was improved with the purchase of a Model B Ford
truck. This was purchased by the city for $125.00 from Spiry and Sons.
The vehicle being a milk truck from Minneapolis, was transformed into a
fire truck, all welding done by Frank Weisgram with the assistance from
other firemen. The Chemical and fire hose equipment were mounted on the
vehicle . The fire department then had a fire truck second to none, in
comparison to the other cities of equal size.

In the spring of 1957, a new truck was purchased from Luverne Fire
Apparatus Co., the fire fighting equipment being completely modern in
every respect, which is still in use today.

The department not only fought fires, but performed other
accomplishments for the Java Community. During the year of 1925, the
department purchased restaurant equipment from an Aberdeen concern.
This was hauled to Java by Bill Moser Sr., which was placed in a
building owned by Jacob Helm. Arend Lakeman Sr., purchased the same and
operated a leading Cafe for many years.

On Friday evening, March 27, 1936, the fire fighters presented a play
"Colored Minstrel" at the Java high school auditorium. The play was
directed by Frank Heezen and music by I. R. Krause. This was a great
success which was enjoyed by all the citizens of Java and surrounding
areas.

The annual fish fry between Java and Mobridge departments began in
1938. This is a joyous and good fellowship affair creating
companionship.

The Java Fire Department of January, 1911 consisted of: Chief, H. J.
Cook; secretary, Ed Aman; Engineers, John Diegel, John Banik and L. L.
Helm; Signal Men, Rochus Aman and F. E. Bauer, Hook and Ladder, I. R.
Krause, Christ Bader and John Spiry; Nozzlemen, Adolph Schafer, L. L.
Helm, J. J. Wolff, John Banik and C. F. Spiry; Horse Cart Men, Philip
Jesser, Jacob Helm, Gottlieb Zeller, Christ Naasz, Arie Cook, Wilson
Hicks, Ed Aman, Adolph Schlepp and Emanuel Schafer.

Fire Chiefs from 1905 to 1974 were: John Hofmeyer, 1905-1911; H. J.
Cook, 1911-1917; Rochus Aman, 1917-1918; Geo. Berreth, 1918-1922; Wm.
Jesser, 1922-1940; Julius Pfitzer, 1940-1958, Jake Teske, 1958-1962;
Julius Pfitzer, 1962-1965; Frank Weisgram, 1965-1966; Bob Goetz,
1966-1974.

Present firemen include: Robert Goetz, Merlyn Melcher, Gerhardt
Fiedler, Russell Bender, Arlo Fiedler, Raymond Goetz, Ron Ketterling,
Julius Pfitzer, James Wilhelm, Mike Darling, Oliver Goetz, Clifford
Grenz, Robert Pfitzer, Fred Walther, Julius Wolf, Marvin Wolf, and
Honorary Members Sam Spiry, Edward Goetz, Louis Horst, Harold Fiedler
and Berthold Schnaible.

                History of the Java School

While the city of Java came into existence in 1900, the history of the
school does not begin until 1901 when the first term of school was held
in a building that had been moved into town from approximately two
miles south of Java. The first term of school was taught by Mrs. Philip
Noble (deceased). There were twenty-three pupils registered for the
first semester and Mrs. Noble received a salary of $35.00 a month.

In 1903 a two-story frame building was built on the site two blocks
east of Main street. In 1907 the population of Java was increasing
rapidly, so it became necessary to build an addition to the south side
of the old structure. Two years of high school was added. There were
only three high school students and Freeman Chute was the principal.

The enrollment of the school increased year after year and in 1920 the
need for expanded classroom facilities became apparent and although the
building was remodeled, it was still inadequate to meet the growing
demand for more educational space. In an effort to meet the pressure, a
small two-room structure was built in 1921 which was used for the
primary classes. In 1920, Java was placed on a three-year accredited
list and a few years later Java high school became a four-year
accredited school.

With E. C. Giffen, school superintendent, leading the pace, and the
fact that the old building was becoming a dangerous fire hazard, the
people of Java began to make plans for the erection of a new fireproof
building and on March 29, 1921 approved the issuance of bonds in the
amount of $60,000 and a new brick building was erected. The new
building was completed in December of that year. Its dimensions were
126 feet long and 66 feet wide with a full basement and two stories.
The building housed a gymnasium which was one of the larger ones in
this area.

The school progressed smoothly in the new building with E. C. Giffen as
superintendent. He was the originator of the Play Day activities for
grade children and was responsible the state superintendent of public
instruction. Giffen was followed by E. H. Halvorson who was
superintendent in 1923 and 1924 and then M. C. Munson was
superintendent for the 1925-26 school term. G. B. Shimmon was the
superintendent in 1926-27-28 and during his tenure much work was done in
landscaping the school grounds.

A. E. Sullivan was superintendent in 1929-30-31. For the next five
years, 1932-33-34-35-36, Frank Heezen was the superintendent and the
school progressed smoothly under his administration.

In 1937 and 1938, Ephraim Renz was the superintendent, and he was
followed by a popular administrator, Dale Summers, from 1939 through
1943.

In 1944, a local man, Harold F. Spiry, became the superintendent and
the school progressed well under his affluent administrative acts. He
served the school in this position through the year 1952.

H. A. Peterson was superintendent in 1953-54. Harold Larson headed the
school in 1955-56 and D. J. Leighty was administrator for the 1957-58
school year.

Vern Cowley came to Java as the superintendent in 1958 and stayed until
1961. It was during his second term that the Java school reorganization
was approved by a 303-75 vote and areas to the south and to the north
in Campbell county became a part of the Java school system.

Charles Singh came to Java in the fall of 1961 and stayed until 1963.
It was during his first term that Java approved a bond issue for
$160,000 for the completion of a new school auditorium and other
improvements on the building which included a new office for the
superintendent, an elementary library, and a new classroom. The new
auditorium seats 720 people.

In 1963, Gerald Nelson became the administrator and was in Java until
1965. The new auditorium was dedicated on January 26, 1964. N. M.
Fisher came to Java in 1965 and stayed for only one term.

In 1966, Stanley B. Raabe became the administrator and has been with
the school system throughout this date. The school has improved
steadily under his able supervision.

During these many years many improvements have been and are still being
made. Adequate educational opportunities still exist in the comfortable
surroundings of the Java school.

        Spacious new school auditorium was added in 1963.
