Larry, born in 1938 is at home with Christ in Selby.

        Mr. and Mrs. Christian Himmerich

                CHRISTIAN HIMMERICH

Christian Himmerich was born in the South Russian area and was married
to Katherina Buechler in 1870. Ten years later Christian and Katherina
Himmerich and family undertook the long journey to come to the United
States. In 1880 they arrived near the Java area and homesteaded about 9
miles east of Java. In 1888, they moved 3 miles from the homestead and
built a farm. Much time was spent in raising cattle and grain, so they
could have enough money to build some more to improve the farm.

In 1901, when Java had begun to grow as a town, Christian moved his
family to Java and got into business in partnership with Christian
Wolff, which they called the Mercantile and Grocery Store. Nine years
he was in business when a heart attack was fatal and he passed away.
The Himmerich family had four children: Rosie, married Ted Kundert;
Jacob, married Kathrine Buechler; Maggie, married Henry Vaagen; and
Kate, married Bert Jones. All the Himmerich children are deceased.

        Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Himmerich

                MR. AND MRS. JACOB HIMMERICH

Jacob Himmerich married Katherine Buechler on Nov. 9, 1909 at the
Buechler church, which was located east of Java. The Himmerichs moved
on his father's farm which was 6 miles east of Java, and is now the
Edwin Oster farm. The couple farmed for many years through the trying
years of drought, storms and grasshopper plague and trying to make a
living for his family. Jacob was active on the Spring Lake school board
and also served on the Telephone Co.

The Himmerichs had five children, who were:

Frieda, married Edwin Oster, who had 4 children and they reside in Java.

Elsie married Alvin Naasz and they had one son and one daughter. Elsie
now resides in Aberdeen, S.D.

Arthur married Lydia Oster and they had children and they reside in
Aberdeen, S.D.

Violet married Otto Bauer and they had 6 children. Violet lives in
Aberdeen, S.D.

Gertrude married Alon Oster and reside in Bremerton, Wash.

In 1942 the Himmerichs bought a house in Java, and moved to town to
live a retired life. Jacob passed away in 1969. Katherine continues to
live in Java and occasionally spends a few months with her daughters in
Aberdeen.

                JOHN HINDEMITH

John Hindemith was born March 19, 1850 in Hoffnungstal Russia. When he
grew to young manhood, he married Christina Weiport of Glueckstal,
South Russia. They were married in 1877 in the Hoffnungstal area. In
1889 the John Hindemith family immigrated to the United States and
settled in the Java area and later homesteaded four miles northeast of
Java. The first school house in that area was called the Hindemith
school, until 1918 when they changed it to the Norbeck school. The
pupils were taught by a Mr. Taylor previously, which was done in the
Hindemith home. The first Lutheran church was also built on the
Hindemith homestead, but later fire overswept the church and a cemetery
was the only remains.

In 1904, the John Hindemith family bought a building which once served
as a school and the Hindemiths added another room and converted to
their home.

Fred, a son was in partnership with Michael Goehring and they owned a
Livery Stable.

John and Christina had 18 children and only two are still living: Mrs.
Fred Schmeichel of McLaughlin, S.D., and Mrs. Carl Berg of Stockton,
Calif.

        The John Hindemith family

        Ignatz Hajsan


                IGNATZ HAJSON

Ignatz Hajson, was a long time resident of the county, who was employed
on farms near Java for many years and was well known in this part of
the country. He came to the United States about 1917. Before he passed
away, he was living at the Eureka Lutheran Home for the aged and at
times stayed at the Julius Pfitzer home when at Java visiting. He died
May 27, 1952 and was buried at Mobridge, May 31, 1952. Pallbearers
were: Julius Pfitzer, Fred F. Naasz, Julius Stangl and Adolph Stangl.

                W. C. HICKS

W. C. Hicks came to Java from Treherne, Manitoba Canada in 1904 to take
over the management of the First State Bank, which had been established
by his brother E. Hicks in connection with the First National Bank of
Aberdeen. Mr. Gannon, President of the Aberdeen bank was for several
years a director of the Java bank.

Mr. Hicks' family, consisting of Mrs. Emily Hicks, sons George Wilson
and Charles Lewis and daughter Ethel, joined him some months later.

Mr. Hicks was president of the bank until his death in 1930. The rest
of the family is now also deceased: Ethel (Mrs. J. K. McGuire) in
Toronto, Canada in 1924; George in Armour, S.D., in 1973; and Lewis in
1965. Mrs. Hicks also died in 1965. Ethel's daughter, Emma Ethel
McGuire was brought up in Java by her grandmother, Mrs. Emily Hicks.

                CHRISTIAN HOCHSTETTER

Christian Hochstetter was born in 1860, in the South Russian territory.
In 1886, he was united in marriage to Margaretha Biel, who was born
Jan. 3, 1864 in Annenthal, South Russia. The Christian Hochstetter
family came to the United States in 1889 and arrived in the Java area,
where they homesteaded 6 miles northeast of Java.

To this union eight children were born:

Fred C., Java; Fredricka (Mrs. George Wahl), Miles City, Mont.; Lena
(Mrs. Wm. Fiechtner), Mobridge; Margaret (Mrs. John Lutz), Java;
Kathryn (Mrs. Ed Gill), Java Louise (Mrs. Leonard Franke), Watertown,
Wisc.; Amelia (Mrs. John Baer), Mandan, N.D.; Edward, Java.

Christian passed away in 1909, and Margaretha remained on the farm with
her family until 1916 when she bought a home in Java and moved her
family to town. She resided in Java until her death on Aug. 26, 1931.

The farm they homesteaded was later occupied by a son Fred, and in more
recent years by Fred's son, Herbert, also deceased. The land is now
owned and farmed by Wilbur Krause.

The two surviving children of the Christian Hochstetters are Mrs.
Kathryn Gill, now a resident at Bethesda Home in Aberdeen, S.D., and
Mrs. Amelia Baer of Mandan, N.D.

        The Christian Hochstetter family

        The Christ R. Hochstatter family

        CHRIST R. HOCHSTATTER

Christ R. Hochstatter was born and raised near the Java area and he was
united in marriage to Rose Spiry in November, 1919 in the Lutheran
church of Java.

The young couple moved on the Spiry homestead and farmed for 2 years
and then moved to the Hochstatter homestead which was located 5 miles
northeast of Java. Many hardships were tolerated by the young couple
while they were farming. The depression and drought forced the
Hochstatters to lose their father's homestead.

In 1940, Christ and Rose and their two children moved to Java where
they bought a home. In 1941, Christ entered the Reisch School of
Auctioneering at Austin, Minn., and received his License in
Auctioneering. Many sales in the South and North Dakota area were
conducted by Christ.

In 1949, Christ was appointed as Chief of Police of Java and served on
the force for 7 years. In 1967 the Hochstatters started an antique shop
with their son, Orville. Five years later it was sold out at auction.
Christ passed away in March 1971 and his wife, Rose passed away in
November 1973.

The two children of the Hochstatters were one daughter, Luella Hammond
has one son and two daughters and reside in Spokane, Wash. One son,
Orville, (Turk) lived with his parents until they passed away and now
resides in Spokane, Wash., where he is employed.

                JOHN HOCHSTETTER

John Hochstetter was born in Neugluckstal, in the state of Odessa,
Russia. It was there he married Katherine Biel and undertook the
journey to come to America in 1886. They spent their honeymoon on the
ship bound for the promised land of America. His bride became sea sick,
but tolerated the trip. After arriving in New York, they traveled by
train to the Dakota Territory. They settled a homestead five miles
northeast of Java. By this time, their money was diminishing and some
hardships just beginning. They lived under a header box which a friend
had found for them until he was able to earn enough money to build a
sod house with the help of friends and neighbors. Earnings were very
cheap in those days such as S2.00 a week. With these earnings he built
his homestead. Hard work and determination was his success as a farmer.

Nine children were born to John and Katherine Hochstetter: John, a
minister; Sophia (Mrs. Wells Tyler); Fred, died young; Barbara; Fred
J.; Katherine (Mrs. Bill Thurn), Bowdle; Christ, an auctioneer, Java;
Magdelina, died young; Lena (Mrs. John Schlepp), Bowdle.

        Mr. and Mrs. John Hochstetter

                SIMON HIRSCH

Simon Hirsch, a son of Anton and Elizabeth (Klein) Hirsch, was born on
Dec. 20, 1856 in Friedensthal, South Russia. He grew up to young
manhood, received his education and made farming his livelihood. In
1878, Simon married Carolina Faul of Friedensthal, South Russia. To this
union, two children were born, which died in infancy and in 1881, his
wife also died. A year later, Simon was remarried to Elizabeth
Friemark, who also came from the Friedensthal area.

In the summer of 1884, Simon and his wife and two children, along with
several more immigrants, sailed on a ship to America. They arrived at
New York City and then traveled by train to the Dakota Territory. They
came to Tripp, S.D., where they remained with friends and relatives
for some time before moving northward, as more land was available in
the northern area.

The Simon Hirsch family arrived in the Sutley area near Campbell
county, and stayed with the Gottlieb Knodel family. A wagon box was
turned upside down and used as sleeping quarters until their sod house
was built on the homestead. Farming was done by hand plowing and hand
seeding and supplies were gotten in the fall of the year. Their main
diet was food made from flour and meat and fruit and dried beans, which
they raised themselves.

In 1894 sorrow again struck this family, the wife passed away and left
Simon with six children to take care of however, within a short time
Simon again remarried. On Nov. 16, 1894, Simon married Julianna (Mohr)
Rieger, who lived in the Hosmer area and was born in Bender, South
Russia. Juliana's parents, Christoph and Willhelmina (Weigart) Mohr,
and brothers had been living in this territory before. Julianna, her
husband, John Rieger, married in 1878, and a family of 7 children
living in Kassel, South Russia, came to America. Five more children were
born to Simon and Julianna, which made it a family of 18 children.

The Hirsch children attended the Buckenberger school in the Thompson
District. School was held in 6 month sessions, and quite a number of
pupils at a wide age range attended a few years, so as they could learn
to read and write.

Winters were very severe and in December, 1888, a severe three-day
blizzard took place, where many lives and livestock was lost. They also
endured several years of drought and an Indian scare was also
experienced.

In 1919, Simon and his wife and 3 remaining daughters moved to Eureka,
but remained there a short time and returned to the farm until 1920,
when they moved to Java. In 1941, they purchased the Henry Giese home
and lived there until 1947, when they moved in with their son, Edward
and wife as their health was failing. Mrs. Hirsch passed away in
August, 1947, and Mr. Hirsch passed away in February, 1948.

        Mr. and Mrs. Simon Hirsch

The children of Simon and Elizabeth were: John, Karl, Jacob of Miles
City, Mont., Magdalina (Mrs. John Fischer) Herreid, all deceased;
Freidaricka (Mrs. Mike Kallas) Bearmouth, Mont.; Mrs. Elizabeth Ritz,
Eureka, S.D. Children of Julianna and John Rieger were: Eva (Mrs.
William Kramlich) Colfax, Wash., Philip Rieger, Portland Ore., John
Rieger, Hebron, N.D., Adolph Rieger, Plevna, Mont., all deceased,
Whilhelmina (Mrs. Andrew Heining) Julia (Mrs. Christian Heinle)
Missoula, Mont., Maria (Mrs. Otto Kundert) Mound City, S.D., all
living. The children of Simon and Julianna were: Gustav, Ida (Mrs.
Christ L. Lutz), Martha (Mrs. Gust A. Rau), Emma (Mrs. Henry Grenz) all
of Eureka, and all living. Edward of Java, S.D. deceased.

        Ed Hirsch family. Standing, Irene, Glenn, Victor, Marion.
        Seated, Mr. and Mrs. Ed Hirsch.

                EDWARD HIRSCH

Edward Hirsch, the second son of Simon and Julianna (Mohr) Hirsch was
born Oct. 6, 1897 at home in the Sutley community. He grew to manhood
on the family farm with the rest of the children. Edward and his
brother Gustav helped their father with the farming until he retired,
because their older brothers and sisters were hired out, got married and
moved to other areas.

Edward married Mary Seif, a daughter of Daniel and Katharina (Bauman)
Seif, born on April 7, 1897 in Ohio and came to this area with her
parents when she was 18 months old. They were married on Oct. 19, 1920
by Rev. Klein of the Baptist church. The young couple lived with a
brother, Gustav on the Hirsch homestead. In the spring of the year, the
young couple bought a house and barn and moved it a short distance from
the homestead. Here they lived, farmed and raised a family through
drought and many bountiful years.

Many times and Ed and Gustav Hirsch families worked together with the
harvesting and haying. They also got together at butchering time when
meat was cured and canned.

In 1928, they had the misfortune of losing their barn and some
livestock in a fire, but soon, with the neighbors help another barn was
built and addition was built onto their previous house. They got their
first radio and telephone in the early thirties, also got a windcharger
and then installed an electric 6-volt system. Drought, dust storms, and
a plague of grasshoppers were all endured by the Hirsch family.

Edward did several different things during his lifetime. He was a
County Assessor for several years. He was also an auctioneer and
shareholder of several livestock sales barns. In his younger years, he
had great interest in baseball and in his retired years, he enjoyed
hunting and fishing.

Early in the winter of 1939, the family moved in with his parents, but
remained a short time and returned to the farm, where they remained
until 1941 when they purchased a home from his parents.

To this union, four children were born: Glenn, married Olga Buechler in
1948 and reside on the home place. They have one son, Roger, married to
Linda Kosters and have one son, Michael. They are presently living on
the farm near his parents. Victor married Allegra Biel in 1950 and own
and operate a farm west of the Glenn Hirsch farm. Irene (Mrs Harold
Goetz) was married in 1947, own and operate a farm 1-1/4 miles east of
Java. They have 2 sons, Jerry operates Goetz Repair and lives at home,
Dale married Cynthia Laible in 1972 and reside at Madison, Neb., where
they both are employed. Marion was married to Harold Mueller in 1947
and passed away in 1948 after an automobile accident.

Edward passed away in November, 1964 and his wife Mary was living alone
until 1974, when she entered the Good Samaritan Nursing Home at Selby.

                CHRISTIAN HORST

Christian Horst, his wife, Elizabeth, his widowed mother Philiphina
Horst, his four sons and daughter came to South Dakota in March of
1898. The sons were Rudolph, Louie, Reinhold and Adolph, the daughter
was Rosina. Fred, the youngest son was born south of Java, in 1903.

The Horst family left South Russia and came to America looking for a
better place to live. They came to New York by ship and traveled by
train to Bowdle, South Dakota. Mr. Horst filed claims on land south of
Java. This was to be their homestead on which they built a sod and rock
house. This house is still standing on the farm which is now owned and
operated by the Merle Noess family. Mr. Horst was also a blacksmith and
had his own shop on the farm and later was employed in a blacksmith
shop in Java.

Christian Horst passed away on the farm in 1908. His mother passed away
in 1909 and Christian's wife, Elizabeth remained on the farm with her
sons until the farm was sold in 1910. Mrs. Horst moved to Java and 29
years later she passed away.

Rosina Horst married Fred Dufloth in February, 1906. The young couple
farmed south of Java until 1920 when they moved to Java, where Mr.
Dufloth and Louie Horst built the Grand Theatre and were in business
for ten years then sold it and moved to Terry, Mont. Fred Dufloth
passed away in 1959 and his wife Rosina passed away in 1964.

Rudolph Horst married Rosina Dufloth in February, 1906 and this couple
farmed on the homestead until it was sold and then they moved to Java.
He operated a dray line and also was town marshal for a number of
years. Rosina passed away in 1918 and was remarried in 1919 to Magdaline
Weisfenning. They moved on a farm north of Java, where he remained
until he passed away in 1929. His wife resides in California.

        Christian Horst family in 1904. Front, Mrs. Philiphina Horst,
        Elizabeth, Christian Horst holding Fred; back, Adolph Horst,
        Reinhold Horst, Louie Horst.

Louie Horst married Emilia Pressler and lived on a farm south of Java
until 1920 when they moved to Java. Louie and Fred Dufloth built and
operated the Grand Theatre. Two years later Louie sold his share and
started working for the Milwaukee railroad. He worked for the railroad
until his retirement in 1958. Mrs. Horst passed away in 1956 and Louie
resides in Java and during the winter he lives with his daughter in
Seattle, Wash.

Reinhold Horst was married to Magdalina Schock in 1916. Mrs. Horst
passed away in 1934 and Reinhold continued farming south of Java and
later east of Java until 1948 when they moved to Glenham. Reinhold
remarried in 1936 to Ernestine Pressler and they now reside in Glenham.

Adolph Horst married Christina Huber in 1920. They farmed in the Java
area for a number of years and later moved to Montana where he farmed
for several years before returning back to Java. Adolph and Christina
live a retired life in Java.

Fred Horst was born on the family homestead in 1903. He lived and
worked with his brother, Reinhold all his life until his death in 1973.

        The Leonard Horst family.

                LEONARD HORST

Leonard Horst was born Dec. 5, 1864 in Hoffnungstal, South Russia. He
married Christina Hindemith on Nov. 2, 1889, who also lived in the
Hoffnungstal area. Leonard and Christina and their adopted
daughter, Magdelena Gehring came to America on March 10, 1898. They
traveled to the Dakota Territory and homesteaded northeast of Java. They
experienced many hardships while farming. They lived on the homestead
until 1917 when they sold it to Fredrich Naasz and moved to Java, where
they bought a home .

Leonard passed away June 18, 1928 at the age of 64 years and his wife
continued living in Java. Christina later remarried, to George Schock
and they remained living in Java. Mrs. Schock passed away a few years
later and Christina made her home with her daughter, Mrs. Charlie Goetz
until she passed away in 1939 at the age of 75 years.

                MR. AND MRS. CARL JACOBS

Carl Jacobs was born in 1898 at Kaukauna, Wis. In 1908, he moved with
his parents to Green Bay, Wisconsin. In 1917, at the age of 19, Carl
followed his sister, Eva Jacobs Bauer, to Java. Having received
training in carpentry at a vocational school in Green Bay, he began
working with a building crew. He helped build the Helm and Moser
houses, some garages, as well as several farm buildings in the area. In
1921, Carl became a rural mail carrier. At this time mail was delivered
by horse and buggy.

In 1924 Carl married Rose Mary Savelsberg. Rose was born in 1906 in
Sheldon, lowa, and had moved with her parents, Mr. and Mrs. Frank
Savelsberg, to Glenham, S.D. Rose went to high school there and then
on to college at N.S.C. for two years.

Carl and Rose had five children: Derry of Independence, lowa, Mrs.
Reginald (Ardelle) Dougherty of Aberdeen, S.D., Mrs. John (Marilyn)
Bohan of Corvallis, Oregon, Jerome of Pierre S.D., and Paul of Java.

Cari continued as a rural mail carrier, a total of 43 years, until his
death due to a heart attack in 1964. His wife, Rose, died of lung
cancer the following year.

                JOHN JOB

John Job was born March 17, 1886 at Kronthal, South Russia to Martin
and Margaretha (Helm) Job. In June of 1906 he immigrated to the United
States and worked on a farm near Pollock, S.D., for 6 months when he
moved to a farm 6 miles north of Java. On October 30, 1912 he married
Justina Doerr at Eureka, S.D. The young couple established a home and
family north of Java. They struggled through many hardships, but the
greatest blow came in May of 1927 when an infant son and Mrs. Job both
succumbed leaving children ranging in age from 3 years to 12 years. Mr.
Job continued farming and in 1928 he married Rosina Mohr of Hosmer, S.
D. This family managed to survive the "Dirty Thirties" eking out a
living as best they could and then retired to the City of Java in 1944
where they lived a quiet and serene retirement. Mr. Job was called to
rest in September of 1956 and Mrs. Job in December of 1972.

Mr. Job was very active in church and school affairs and was often
called upon to read Scripture in the absence of Rev. Kirchdoerfer, who
served the rural congregation that was attended by the entire family
in fact there was never an excuse for any member of the family not to
attend Sunday School and Church services every Sunday even though the
family had to ride triple deck in their 1928 Chevrolet. Mr. Job also
was very much in favor of education and saw to it that his children
attended school regularly in spite of snow and bitter cold requiring a
horse drawn sled, sheepskin coats and mountains of quilts to get there.

The children are as follows: Tillie, Robert, Minnie, Frieda (Mrs.
Oliver McCauley), Ervin, Rueben, LaRayne (Mrs. Gilbert Schlepp), Vina
(Mrs. Charles York) and Delphia (Mrs. George Trudeau).

Robert and Hulda (Forkel), Aberdeen, children Roberta Gaylon and
Sharlene; Frieda and Oliver McCauley, Portland, Ore., one child,
Kathie; Ervin and Janice, Wasco, Calif., two children, Michael and
Barbara; Rueben and Darlene (Kurle), Selby, S.D., two sons, Paul and
Jason; LaRayne and Gilbert Schlepp, Java, S.D., two daughters, Barbara
and Carolyn; Vina and Charles York, Cudahy, Wis. three children,
Cynthia, Jon and Dale, Delphia and George Trudeau, Cudahy, Wisc., three
children, Kevin, Keith and Lyle.

        Mr. and Mrs. Friedrich Job

                FRIEDRICH JOB

Friedrich (Fred) Job was born at Kronthal, South Russia on December 24,
1891 to Martin and Marguerite (Helm) Job. Fred had four sisters and
three brothers and of this family, three migrated to the United States;
a brother, John and a sister, Christina. John and Fred settled in the
Java area, and Christina in the Hosmer area.

Fred immigrated to the United States in 1911 at the age of 20 years and
was employed on farms near the Java area. Enlisting in the United
States Army in 1918, Fred served with Company M of the 64th Infantry in
France. He received his discharge in 1919 and returned to the Java
area. Again he found employment and on March 18, 1920 he was married to
Philipina Doerr of Eureka, S.D. The Jobs moved on a farm near Java,
and farmed several years, and later moved to a farm 4 miles northwest
of Java.

Six children were born to Fred and Philipina, who were:

Milbert, who passed away in 1954.

Amanda married Herbert Schneider and own and operate a farm north of
Java. They have 3 sons, Dean, employed and resides in Denver, Colo.
Dennis, now touring the western states. Jerry, attending college in
Brookings, S.D.

Esther married Ole Naasz and they own and operate Ole's T.V. and reside
in Mobridge, S.D. They have two daughters, Vickie, married Eugene
Locatelli, who is in the Service, and stationed in Cheyenne, Wyo.,
Gwen married Archie Bauman, employed and resides in Mobridge, S.D.
One son David, still at home and attending Junior High School. Oscar
married Marge Young and reside in Rapid City, S.D.

Albert married Marie and reside in Kallispell, Mont. They have one son,
Jeffery, and one daughter, Jenny, both attending school.

Margaret married James Anderson, who is office manager of Brown County
ASCS in Aberdeen, S.D. They have one son, Myron, and two daughters,
Carol and Connie, who are attending school in Aberdeen.

                VALENTINE KARY SR.

Mr. and Mrs. Valentine Kary Sr., both born in Landau, South Russia,
decided to make a long journey to America, in 1907, arriving in the
town of Java, in January, 1908, with a large family, consisting of 6
boys and 4 girls, another son born and died in Russia. and one son was
born in America.

The journey started with the entire family till to Germany, then one
daughter (Katherine) married, had to stay due to illness of husband. No
one has ever seen her since although the parents received a few letters
the first few years .

The rest of the family continued the journey to New York there the next
oldest daughter (Rose) and family continued on while the rest of the
Kary family remained two months due to illness, then continued their
voyage on to the Dakotas. Jacob Aman Sr., brother of Mrs. Kary, met
them with a bob sled and brought them to Java, where they made their
home with the Amans for two years.

The Kary family built their first new home in America in Campbell
county. The home was built of mud and straw and labor was done by
father and sons and help from Jacob Aman and sons. The house consisted
of three rooms. The Kary home was located 12 miles north and 1/2 mile
west of Java. They lived in this home for a number of years.

Later the Karys homesteaded northeast of Java, now the Rueben G.
Eberhardt farm, and remained on this farm until their children were
grown and married. Then, Mr. and Mrs. Kary decided to retire and moved
to Eureka, S.D.

           Mr. and Mrs. Valentine Kary, Sr.

The Kary family were: Reinhold, married and resides in Eureka; Jacob
married and resides in Eureka; Mike married and resides in Java; John,
deceased; Valentine Jr. deceased; Emanuel married and resides in
Eureka; Albert, the son born in America, resides in Java, Katherine
deceased; Rose (Heinrick) resides at Herreid, S.D.; Elizabeth
(Eberhardt) deceased; Lydia (Schneider) deceased.

Mr. Kary, while he suffered the hardships of pioneering, his friendly
spirit and ever-ready smile won for him a place in the hearts of
residents of the community. He was a devoted father and a devout member
of the German Baptist Church of Eureka, until his death at the age of
87 years.

                EMANUEL R. KARY FAMILY

Emanuel R. Kary was born on April 9, 1903 in South Russia, the son of
Valentine and Maria Kary. Gertrude Flemmer Kary was born on June 5,
1909 in Eureka, S.D., the daughter of Philipp and Katharina Flemmer.
The two were united in marriage on June 14, 1928.

Mr. and Mrs. Emanuel R. Kary purchased the Louie Gershin Store in Java,
in 1941. Here they were members of the Java Lutheran Church, Gertrude
serving as president of the Ladies Aid, teaching Sunday school and
Bible school. Mr. Kary, known as "Mandy" was an active member of the
Java Chamber of Commerce, serving as president. He was also active in
civic affairs and served on projects dealing with improvements of the
City.

In 1951, the Karys sold their store in Java, known as Kary's Market, to
Gilbert and LaRayne Schlepp and moved to Mobridge, S.D., where Mandy
worked as a salesman for the Mobridge Wholesale Company. Later he
became a car salesman for the Davidson Buick Garage. In 1956, the Karys
returned to Java and purchased the Red Owl Store from Elmer Biel. They
sold this store in 1959 to Marvin Pfitzer.

The Karys have three sons: Richard E., who graduated from the Java High
school in 1949. He is a Shell Jobber in Albert Lea, Minn., having four
daughters; Dean P., graduated from the Java high school in 1952. He is
a Freight Agent for the Santa Fe Railways in Dodge City, Kans. Dean has
four sons, one daughter and one granddaughter. Merlin J. graduated from
Mobridge High school. He is a computer programmer, lives in Virginia
Beach, Virginia, and has one daughter.

Mr. and Mrs. Emanuel R. Kary have made their permanent home in
Eureka, S.D.

                MICHAEL KARY FAMILY

In 1897, a son was born to Mr. and Mrs. Valentine Kary at South Russia.
At the age of 11 years, Michael, with his parents came to America to
join the rest of their friends and relatives.

Michael's first homestead was with the Jake Aman family, which is
presently owned and operated by Mr. and Mrs. Albert Aman, which is
located 7 miles northeast of Java. This was the family home until
spring, when they moved to various other farms in the Java area.
Michael grew to manhood in the Java area and worked as a farmhand for
many years. On Oct. 9, 1919 Michael married Martha Biel who was the
daughter of Mr. and Mrs. George Biel Sr., who had homesteaded 6 miles
north of Java, now the Herbert Schneider farm.

After their marriage, the young couple settled on a farm 9 miles
northeast of Java, which is now owned and operated by the LeRoy
Sandmeier family. Three children were born to Michael (Mike) and
Martha, they were a daughter, Claudia (Mrs. Jim Marchewik), a son Alvin
(deceased) and a younger son, Wilbur, married Lorraine Boschee and
reside on a farm northeast of Java.

The Michael Kary family moved on various farms around the Java area
until they retired. In 1950, the Karys purchased a home in Java where
they presently reside. They operated a creamery for 8 years and then
Mike became owner and operator of Mike's Salvage, while his wife Martha
was an inspiring housewife. Many years later Mike and Martha helped
their daughter operate Claudia's Cafe for 2-1/2 years.

Even though many hardships and sad hours were endured by the couple,
they are still happily married and are enjoying a retired life with
their 7 grandchildren and 4 great grandchildren.

        Mr. and Mrs. Michael Kary

                GEORGE KIRCHDOERFER FAMILY

Rev. George Kirchdoerfer served St. Paul's Lutheran church of Java for
40 years. He arrived in Java from Germany in May, 1902. A year later,
Mrs. Kirchdoerfer, nee Roth, arrived from Germany and the young couple
was married at Marquette, Wis., on June 24, 1903.

When Rev. Kirchdoerfer came to Java, the St. Paul's congregation
consisted of eight families. The church building was small in structure
but in a few years was replaced with a new structure, 28x60 in size.
The original church building was used as a parochial school. The first
parsonage was only a two room affair, but in 1903 a new parsonage was
built and it was again enlarged in 1915.

        The Kirchdoerfer family. Left to right, Rev. George
        Kirchdoerfer, Waldemar, Herbert, Minnie, Mrs. Kirchdoerfer,
        Herman.

In addition to serving the Java congregation, Rev. Kirchdoerfer also
served in other towns in Walworth county, as well as Edmunds county,
serving seven congregations in all. He estimated that he traveled
25,000 miles with his ponies while serving these congregations.

The Java congregation numbered 140 families when he retired in May of
1942. At that time, he and Mrs. Kirchdoerfer moved to Waverly, Iowa,
where they spent the rest of their days. Rev. Kirchdoerfer passed away
in March 1960 and Mrs. Kirchdoerfer, June, 1972. The Kirchdoerfers had
six children; two, of whom died in infancy. The others presently reside
in the following places: Mrs. Minnie Haase, Rapid City, S.D.;
Waldemar, Austin, Minn.; Herman, San Marino, Calif., and Herbert,
Clinton, Iowa.

        Mr. and Mrs. John Klein and Ida

                JOHN KLEIN

John Klein was born in Glueckstahl, South Russia on Jan. 26, 1892 and
was the son of Peter and Rosina (Schlichter) Kurle. When his mother
married Peter Klein, she was a widow with three small children,
Elizabeth, Jake and Eva Kurle.

As a result of exposure, suffered during a winter storm, Peter Klein
developed pneumonia and never regained his health. He died when John
was a young child. His mother then married a widower, Bratzel. Christ
Bratzel was born to this union. She was widowed for the third time when
Mr. Bratzel died in a threshing accident. Women had no property rights
in Russia and life was very hard for the family. The mother worked as a
midwife, in addition to delivering the new baby, she also was
responsible for the other housework. Her own children stayed with
relatives and John went to live with a childless family and was treated
harshly.

Later his mother remarried to Christian Goetz and a son, Simon Goetz
was born. In 1905, Mr. Goetz brought the family to America. The oldest
daughter remained in Russia as her plans were for marriage. John was 13
years old when they settled in the Lowry-Akaska area. From then on, he
was self supporting. He was employed as a farmhand in the summers and
winters he spent as a janitor for various hotels for room and board.
Later he found employment with the railroad at Wishek, N.D.

John was living with his half-sister Eva and husband, Jake Eisenbeisz,
when he met his future bride. On Jan. 19, 1915, John married Emma
Sonnenfeld, daughter of John and Rosina (Fischer) Sonnenfeld. The young
couple made their home with Eva and Jake Eisenbeisz until 1915, when
they settled on a farm 7 miles northeast of Java. Their daughter, Ida
was born here and they remained here until 1938, when the family moved
to Eureka, where John owned and operated a service station. In 1944,
they moved to Bowdle, and remained in farming until 1958. Their
daughter, Ida and her husband took over the farm. Emma died on Jan. 22,
1972 and John passed away April 8, 1972.

Their daughter, Ida ( Mrs . Herbert Fiechtner ) and husband farmed the
home place until 1972, when their youngest son, Joel and his wife,
Kathy (Mittleider) took over the management of the farm. Their other
children are Phonia ( Mrs. Harvey Diedtrich ) Eureka, S.D., Terry
married Arliss Schilling and live at Titusville, Fla., Connie (Mrs.
Rodney Blumhardt) of Littleton, Colo., John, married and lives at Monte
Vista, Colo., Barbara (Mrs. Norby Reuer) of White River, S.D.

                HENRY KAST

Henry Kast was born Dec. 7, 1885, in Odessa, Russia. He was left an
orphan at an early age. His father passing away when he was 35 and his
mother at the age of 38. This left a family of four surviving boys and
one daughter. One son passed away in infancy.

At the age of 16, Henry came to the United States and worked for his
uncle in Lincoln, Neb., who had a blacksmith shop. When he had earned
enough money he had his brothers, Christ, Edward, Rudolph and his
sister, Katherine come to America. In the states they did various jobs
for people for board and room. In those days there was no A.D.C. or
welfare to take care of the needy, so it was quite a struggle for the
remaining children.

In February, 1908 he married Paulina Buckenberger, who was the daughter
of Andrew and Dorothea (Oswald) Buckenberger, born in Yankton county in
January, 1884. They struggled and saved and eventually bought the
Buckenberger farm in Campbell county near Sutley. For some time his
brothers and sister lived with them.

There were 6 children born to them, two sons and four daughters. The
first 2 children died in infancy. In October 1911 a set of twins were
born, who were Leon now living in Java, and Ida Kast (Rosin) who passed
away in 1972. Five years later, again October 1916, another set of
twins were born to them. These were twin girls and one died in infancy
and the remaining one is Frieda Kast Himrich, living in Mobridge, S.D.

They remained on the farm until February, 1923. Because of ailing
health, they had a farm auction, bought a house in Java and moved to
town. The children finished their education in the Java Public Schools.
Leon graduated in 1932 and in the same year married Nettie Spiry. They
made their home in Java all of their married life. Two sons were born to
them, Adrian married and resides in Haynes, N.D., and Lynn, married
and resides in Elkhart, Ind. Ida graduated in 1929 and in 1936 she
married William Rosin of Selby, S.D., and they had 3 children: Corrine
Rosin (Quenzer) of Park River, N.D., Carol Rosin (Reuer) of rural
Hoven, S.D., and William H. Rosin Jr., of Selby, S.D. Frieda,
graduated in 1935 and married in 1936 in a double wedding with her
sister Ida. They had one daughter, Sharon Himrich (Osborne) of
Brainerd, Minn.

The Henry Kasts again went to farming, but farmed from town until 1951
when they sold their farm to Gust Rau. Henry suffered a heart condition
and passed away in March, 1957 at the age of 71 years. Mrs. Kast
continued to reside in Java, until health failed and she spent some
time with the children and in a Nursing Home in Mobridge, S.D., until
her death in February, 1963 at the age of 79 years.

        Henry Kast family. Leon, Mr. Kast. Front, Frieda, Mrs. Kast,
        and Ida.

They were members of the Reformed Church at Sutley and at Java.

They went through many hardships during the drought and grasshopper
plague, but never lost faith in this country. Mr. Kast would often
relate his younger days when he worked in the blacksmith shop. Many
nights were spent in the cold with only a coat to cover and the forge
was his only source of heat. There were many nights he was so hungry he
couldn't sleep.

Most of the things were done together on the farm. At harvest time Mrs.
Kast would help in the fields as most women were accustomed to doing.
Milking 16 cows by hand was not unusual.

                MR. AND MRS. JOHANN KURLE

Johan and Eva (Braun) Kurle came to the United States from Bergdorf,
South Russia in 1899. They both were born about 1838 in Bergdorf.
Johann was the son of Karl and Rosina (Leicht) Kurle. Johann
homesteaded land northwest of Java in 1906, which is presently owned by
Emanuel Sieler.

They had eight children, all deceased: Johann, who died in Russia, Eva,
(Mrs. Frederick Wolf) and Christian, both of Java, Elizabeth (Mrs.
Adam Merkel) of Greenway; Jacob of Bowdle; David, who lived in the
Java and McLaughlin areas; and twins, Rosina, who died in infancy and
Magdalena (Mrs. Christoph Huber), of Akaska.

Mr. Kurle passed away March 5, 1910, and his wife, Eva, on January 2,
1911.

                MR. AND MRS. CHRISTIAN KURLE

Christian and Karolina (Hafner) Kurle and family entered the United
States at Boston, Mass., October 25, 1907 on the vessel S. S. Saxonia.
Christian, a son of Johann and Eva Kurle, was born in Bergdorf, South
Russia on July 18, 1862. His wife, Karolina, was born at Kassel, South
Russia, January 2, 1868. They were married June 20, 1889 at Bergdorf.

After arriving in America the family lived on various farms in the
Java-Selby area and in 1921 purchased the land that Johann had
homesteaded, living there until ill health forced them to enter the
Eureka Lutheran Home, where they both passed away, Karolina on June 25,
1941 and Christian on December 23, 1944.

They had nine children, Lydia (Mrs. Bert Reynolds) of Wheaton, Minn.,
and later of California, where she passed away; Eva, (Mrs. William
Evers) of Aberdeen; Caroline, of Java; Christian Jr., of Selby, Adolph,
Rudolph, Robert, Martha (Mrs. John Kapfenstein) all of Java; and Bertha,
(Mrs. Philip Fischer) of Renton, Wash. Christian, Robert, Martha and
Bertha are the only living children.

        Mr. and Mrs. Christian Kurle

                GERARD KOSTERS

Gerard Kosters (better known as George) was born at Ottersum, Holland.
When he went to Germany at the age of 15, he worked in the coal mines.
He left Germany in 1893, and came to America, arriving in DePere, Wis.,
working in the paper mills. He came to Hoven, South Dakota, where he met
his future wife, Maria Theunissen, who was born at Mook Netherlands,
July 23, 1874. She came to America when she was a young girl with her
parents.

Mr. Kosters lived on land southeast of Java, near Spring Lake. Here he
dug a well, built a house and barn across the valley. Driving his horse
and buggy all the way to Aberdeen, he got married Nov. 1, 1898, and
they came back to his farm and started their lives together.

In the spring of 1907, they moved their house and farm buildings to the
present location. Later, in 1916 he built a big new part on the house.
Here is where he resided until the time of his death on July 5, 1925.
After the death of Mr. Kosters, his son, Peter ran the farm. In 1936,
Garret Kosters purchased the home place and at present no one lives on
the farm, but the land is now owned by John Kosters.

Mrs. Kosters passed away, May 22, 1929, leaving ten children:
Wilhelmina, (Mrs. Gottlieb Schuh), deceased; Antonia, deceased;
Gertrude, (Mrs. Steve Bruckner Sr.) of Aberdeen; Maria, (Mrs. George
Sebert Sr.) of Java; Peter Henry, Garret and John, all of Selby,
Antonia, (Mrs. Frank Stefanich) deceased; Elizabeth, Mrs. Ladis Raba) of
Selby, Katherine, (Mrs. Martin Raba) of Selby.

        The Johanas Krause family. Back, Bertha, Katherine, Israel C.
        Front, Alex, John, Lydia, Louise.

                JOHANAS KRAUSE FAMILY

The reason most pioneers came to America was discontentment with their
life in the "Old Country" and to find and settle new land. Such was the
case with Johanas Krause. He sold all his possessions in Russia and
came to America, looking for farm land to provide a livelihood and a
place to settle, where he and his descendants would be happy and
content. John must have chosen his site very well, as the homestead
they selected has remained in the Krause family for 86 years, where
some of the original buildings, including the barn, are still being
used.

John Krause was born September 14, 1865, in South Russia, and was a son
of Karl Krause and his wife, nee Minnie Reich. On December 18,. 1886,
he was married to Louise Weidenlich, who was born March 20, 1868 at
Friedensthal, Roumania, where she was baptized. Louise was confirmed in
1882 in Arzis. This union was blessed with seven children, two of whom,
a daughter and a son died in infancy; the other two sons and three
daughters being: Katherine, Israel C., Bertha, Alex and Lydia.

The family came to the United States in 1889, settling on a farm five
miles north of Java, where the youngest five of their children were
born. Here, the family worked together and endured the hardships,
misfortunes and illnesses that were typical of all their pioneer
neighbors.

After living on their farm for 46 years, in 1935, Mr. and Mrs. Krause
built a home in Java, and moved to town. After an illness of about two
years, Mrs. Krause, 69, passed away at her home on Friday, March 11,
after being confined to a wheel chair for several months. On Sunday,
December 23, 1956, after a long illness, following a stroke, John
Krause, 91, died at the home of his daughter, Mrs. Wm. (Lydia) Frank in
Java, where he had been making his home.

                Katherine
Katie Krause was born April 10, 1890, on the farm north of Java, where
she grew up and attended school. On October 29, 1912, she was married
to Christ M. Haux at Java, and they made their home on a farm, 2 miles
northwest of Java. Her husband passed away on June 29, 1942, and she
continued to live on the farm until 1945, when she moved to town. After
spending some time in Nursing Homes and with relatives, she passed away
on her 78th birthday, April 10, 1968. She was the mother of four sons
and two daughters: Robert, of Salmon, Idaho, married Frieda Goetz;
Wilson (deceased); Herman H., who lives north of Java, married Verna
Laemmle, and has three children, Gene, Candice and Darcy Herbert, who
lives northwest of Java, married Esther Haux and has one daughter,
Mercedes; Evelyn, married Fred Johnle and they reside in Aberdeen; and
Bertha, who married Ray Kasper, lives in Whitefish, Mont., and has
three children, LeAnn, Gregory and Raymond.

                Israel C.
Israel C. Krause was born May 19, 1891 on the farm north of Java, where
he attended rural school and lived all his life. On October 8, 1935, he
married Lydia Schilling at Bowdle, and they made their home on the
family homestead, north of Java, until his death on Tuesday, September
12, 1973, at the age of 82. Israel was the father of three children,
Harlan living north of Java, married Ramona Buechler of Bowdle and has
four children, Marcia, Marlys, Sheila and James, Harry, living with his
mother on the homestead, north of Java; and Stella, who married Harlan
Buechler of Bowdle where they live on a farm, has three children, Max,
Mavis and Marilyn.

                Bertha
Bertha Krause was born September 5, 1893, on the homestead north of
Java, where she grew to womanhood. She was confirmed on March 20, 1910,
and was united in marriage to William Frank of Aberdeen, on May 12,
1921. The couple lived in Aberdeen for some time; went to Brookings,
where Mr. Frank attended school; then returned to Java after she
underwent an operation at an Aberdeen hospital on January 27. She
passed away Tuesday evening, January 31, 1923, at the age of 29 years.
She was the mother of two sons: Art, who lives in Java; and Wes, who
married Ruth Kurle, lives in Java, and has two children, Clyde and
Colleen.

                Alex
Alex Krause was born December 22, 1896 at the homestead north of Java,
where he grew up and attended school. Much of his youth was plagued
with illness. He spent all his life on farms north of Java, except for
the 14 months he spent in the Navy, enlisting in 1918. On June 22,
1931, he married Mary Boettcher at Selby. They moved from their farm
north of Java, into town in 1964, where they still reside. They had one
son, Wilbur, who married Mary Anne Anderson; and they live on a farm
north of Java, with their seven children: Cynthia, Chloe, Catherine,
Bill, Bob, Ben and Carla.

                Lydia
Lydia Krause was born December 7, 1898 on the farmstead north of Java,
where she spent her childhood. She married William Frank on February
12,1930, and they made their home in Java, where she is presently
living. Her husband passed away on April 9, 1972. They were the parents
of two sons: Curtis, who lives in Coos Bay, Ore., married Betty Mangal,
and they have a daughter and son, Sharon and Robert; Earl, married
Maxine Haux, and they live with their son, Douglas in Rapid City.

                THE I. R. KRAUSE FAMILY

Mr. and Mrs. Israel R. Krause and their parents were among Java's
earliest residents.

I. R. Krause was born in Bessarabia, South Russia of German parentage
in 1882 and came to the United States with his parents, Karl and
Wilhelmina (Reich) Krause and brother, Fred and sister, Ledia in 1890.
The family settled on a homestead near the farmstead of another older
son, John, who with his wife, Louise, had come to the United States
earlier.

        Karl Krause family (L-R): Karl, Fred, Ledia, Israel R., and
        Wilhelmina.

I. R. Krause attended rural school near his home and after completing
the 8th grade taught that school for a number of years. In 1904, he and
his brother, Fred, established the Krause Mercantile Store, one of
Java's first business places. He also did some barbering in his early
years and was a member of one of Java's first dance orchestras. He
possessed unusual natural musical ability,--and never took a lesson
except a few by correspondence course, and could play practically any
instrument by ear as well as by notes.

In 1913 he was named postmaster at Java and operated it in the north
side of the former Krause Mercantile store building along with a
jewelry and clock and watch repair business, which was also
self-taught. At that time, the Krause Mercantile store was
discontinued. As a sideline he also sold made-to-measure suits for men
for many years as well as phonographs and records. Later he built the
present Java postoffice building. In the fall of 1924, the local
telephone switchboard was also moved to the postoffice building and
operated from there for a number of years. After serving as postmaster
for 23 years, he was replaced in that office by J. S. Pfitzer. Mr.
Krause then devoted full time to his favorite vocation, that of tuning
and fixing pianos and organs, serving a large area in this part of
South Dakota as well as North Dakota. He could repair or tune almost
any instrument and also did sewing machine adjusting.

He served as Justice of Peace of Java for many years. One of the
anecdotes told about him while he was serving in that office was
typical. A traffic offender had been brought before him and after the
case, he told the defendant that the fine was $25. "Oh, I've got that
right in my pocket," quipped the man. "And one day in jail!" continued
Mr. Krause, "Have you got that in your pocket, too?" He also served as
Mayor of Java for two terms in the 1950's, and was a lifetime member of
the Java Fire Department.

He was a member of the Java and Selby bands for many years and directed
the Java school band for several years while serving as dean of the
Java school dormitory in the 1940's. He served as organist of St. Paul
Lutheran church for 33 years and as choir director for many of those
years. He also organized the first Little German Band at Java. Members
of the original group were: Sam and Philip H. Spiry, August C. and
Gilbert Schlepp, Gerhard Bauman, and Mr. Krause and daughter Cecelia
and son, Levi. Of that group only Sam Spiry, Gerhard Bauman, Cecelia
and Levi still survive. For many years he also served as secretary of
the Modern Woodmen of America lodge at Java.

On Nov. 14,1905 he married Anna Mary Diegel, and they spent the
remainder of their lives in Java. The house in which they lived and
raised their family, still stands. It is now owned by Mr. and Mrs.
George Loebs. Mr. Loebs is a native of Bowdle, and Mrs. Loebs, the
former Dorris Brown, grew up at Glenham.

Mrs. Krause was born in 1885 on a farm near the eastern edge of
Campbell county south of Eureka, a daughter of Fred and Anna Maria
Diegel, and the family underwent many of the hardships of those early
pioneer days. At the age of 13 she moved with her family to a farm
northwest of Java and in 1902 to Java, where they operated the Java
Hotel for a number of years, Mrs. Krause serving as the chief cook
though she was only a teenager. After her marriage she was active in
St. Paul Lutheran church and was one of the early members of the Ladies
Aid of the church. She and her husband were also members of a Singing
Club for many years,--a group of couples who would gather on a Sunday
evening for hymn singing, with Mr. Krause serving as accompanist. She
was also a member of the Sewing Club now known as the Thimble Club. She
and Mr. Krause were the parents of eight children. Ida, Cecelia, Levi,
Geraldine, Leota, Orpheus, and Patricia. One, Titus, died while a small
boy. Mrs. Krause died Sept. 19, 1938, after a long illness.

In 1945, Mr. Krause married Mrs. Katie (Haux) Pfaff of Java, and they
continued to make their home in the family residence. Mr. Krause died
on May 4, 1966 and Mrs. Krause died Oct. 15, 1969. She had two
children, Arthur Pfaff of Conde, and Mrs. Elmer (Clara) Biel of Howard.

Ida Krause married Fritz Clement of Java, and they resided in Java for
many years where they published the Java Herald. In 1949 they moved to
Selby, having purchased the Selby Record in 1945, and where they still
publish both papers. Their son, Bruce is associated in the business with
them, and they also have a daughter, Germaine (Bunny), now Mrs. Wendel
Mead, who with her two daughters, Mary and Linda, resides in Bowie,
Maryland; her husband being employed as a Computer Program Systems
analyst with the Internal Revenue Service in Washington, D. C.

Cecelia Krause married William Elsing of Aberdeen, who died in 1967,
and she still resides in Aberdeen, where she does income tax work and
is a part-time bookkeeper. She has a step-daughter, Ruth Gallipo,
Aberdeen, and four grandchildren.

        Mr. and Mrs. Israel R. Krause--1905

Levi Krause married Irene Fiechtner and they reside at Mobridge, where
he recently retired from the watch repair business. The family also
operated the Krause Drive-In for many years and sold it this year. They
have four children: Russell, who is a consulting engineer for Charles
T. Main Co., of Boston. He and his wife, Margaret and four children
live at Framingham, Mass. Judy, who is a part-time teacher in biology,
is married to Jim Halamka, who teaches in Junior College. They and
their three sons live at Port Huron, Mich. Kenneth, who is employed
with the State Highway Department as an engineer in soils and research.
He and his wife Beverly live at Pierre. Lorna is a part-time teacher
and she and her husband, Ed Watkins live on a ranch near St. Lawrence.

Geraldine, married to Howard Richardson, who worked for many years as a
welder for Reynolds Aluminum, now retired, and they live at Kelso,
Wash. They have six children: Maureen, married to Dean Smith, and they
have two children and live at Loomis, Calif. Mary Elin, married to Alan
Peterson, they have two children and live at Centralia, Wash. Jerimay,
married to James Donaldson; they have one child and live at Vancouver,
Wash. Cecelia, married to Don Creech; they have two children and live
at Cassville, Mo. Michael, and his wife, Carol and one child live at
Eugene, Ore., where he is attending school. Joe and his wife, Julie live
at Vancouver, Wash.

Leota, a beauty operator, is married to Harvey Marshall and they now
live a retired life in a resort area near Alpena, Mich., after
operating a marina for many years on the Huron river near Belleville,
Mich. Their son, Paul and his wife, Gail live at Grand Ledge, Mich.
Paul is employed with the telephone company at Lansing, Mich.

Orpheus, married Dorothy Bonen. He worked as an administrative officer
for many years for the U. S. Bureau of Animal Industry and she as a
secretary for U. S. Social Security Administration. Both are now
retired. They live at Jacksonville, Fla. They have three children:
Jacquelyn, married to Major Don Seaborn of the U. S. Air Force,
presently stationed in Germany. They have two sons. Robbin, Lt.
Commander with the U. S. Coast Guard at Great Lakes, Ill. He and his
wife, Barbara, and two children live at Buffalo Grove, Ill. Timothy,
who is director of the Jacksonville, Fla., City Zoo. He and his wife,
Josie and two children live in Jacksonville. A foster daughter, Julie,
also lives with Orpheus and his wife.

Patricia, married to Merle Wolf and they live at Mobridge. Merle is
employed as a salesman for Rabenberg Implement Inc., at Selby. They
have three children: Susan, who taught music for three years at Leola,
now is employed as parish worker for Grace Lutheran church at
Watertown. Pamela and David at home.

                ADAM LORENZ

Adam Lorenz was born Jan. 11, 1870 at Odessa South Russia, where he
grew to manhood. He was united in marriage to Anna Marie Pohl, who was
born on May 9, 1872 at Odessa, South Russia.

The young couple, with their eldest daughter, Emily, migrated to the
United States, aboard the ship (Mayflower) and arrived at New York Port
on Dec. 1, 1901. From New York, they journeyed by train to the South
Dakota Territory. The Adam Lorenz family settled at Emery, S.D., where
Adam found employment at a shoe repair shop.

In 1914, Adam and Anna Marie, along with their seven children, moved to
the Java area, where they homesteaded on a farm north of Java, which is
presently owned and operated by the Joe Heilman family. Four more
children were born to the Adam Lorenz family. The oldest daughter,
Emily, passed away in 1918 and one son, George, passed away in 1922. A
twin, Marie Ann died a few weeks after birth and the youngest son,
William died in infancy.

        Mr. and Mrs. Adam Lorenz

The children of the Adam Lorenz family were: Emily, born Dec. 24, 1898
at Russia, and passed away in 1918. Emanuel, born Dec. 12, 1901;
married Mary Kranzler. Emanuel passed away in 1968 and his wife
remarried and now resides in Aberdeen, S.D. Nine children were born to
this family, who were: Emil, died in infancy, Martha (Mrs. Elmer
Schmierer) reside on the Michael Schmierer homestead, have one daughter
Rosanna (Mrs. Dan Holsworth) own and operate the Hoven Review print
shop, have one son, Chad, and reside at Hoven, S.D. Richard married
and now is a retired serviceman living in New Mexico. Viola, died in
infancy; Erna, unmarried, died at the age of 20 years. Howard, died at
the age of 9 years; Bernice (Mrs. Marvin Walther) resides at Westport,
S.D., have 3 daughters and 4 sons, who are attending schools at
Aberdeen. Joyce was married to Clarence Schlepp; now employed at Hayes
Bros. Printing, resides at Aberdeen, S.D., has 3 daughters, attending
Aberdeen schools. Irene, unmarried, employed at Walgreen Drug of
Aberdeen, where she resides. Carol (Mrs. Ralph Hilgeman) resides at
Wetonka, S.D., have 2 sons attending Aberdeen schools, born June 29,
1903. Tillie, married Carl Oster and reside at Selfridge, N.D., have 6
sons: Herbert, Harold, Harvey, Garold, Carl Jr., and Ervin and 2
daughters, Betty and Anna Marie, all married. George Adam born April 5,
1905, unmarried, died at the age of 17 years. Anna Marie born Dec. 6,
1907, married Fred Strieb and reside at San Bernardino, Calif., have 4
daughters Lorraine, Dallas, Dorothy and Romona and 2 sons, Marvin and
Delmar, all married. Jacob born Nov. 10, 1909, married and resides at
Vancouver, Wash., has 2 daughters, Twila and Lynda, both married.
Christine, born Nov. 9, 1911, married Raymond Kurle and reside at
Spokane, Wash.; have 2 sons, Delmar and Leroy and 2 daughters Joyce and
Romona all married. John born Dec. 5, 1913, married Virginia Biock
(widowed) resides at Spokane, Wash., have 2 daughters, both married.
Elizabeth, twin, born June 8, 1915, married Ezra Haeger and reside in
Spokane, Wash., have 2 sons, Clyde (deceased) and Virgil and one
daughter Betty, both are married. Marie Ann, twin, born June 8, 1915,
died several weeks after birth. William born March, 1917, died shortly
after birth.

Adam and Anna Marie had 35 grandchildren and 74 great grandchildren and
several great, great grandchildren.

        Miklos family. Left to right, Ida, Sophia (mother), Louis (Loy)
        Miklos II, Joseph, Esther, Ella.

                LOUIS MIKLOS II

Louis (Loy) Miklos II, was born to Louis and Sophia (Benedick) Miklos
in Feloer Vuros Dob Vas Meyge, Hungary on Feb. 27, 1885. Six children
were born to this couple, Louis (Loy) II was the second child and one
brother and 4 sisters and one sister, Julie, deceased.

Sophia, Louis' mother, came with friends to the United States in 1893.
They settled at Bangor, South Dakota, where she sought employment at
the Buchanan Hotel. A year later Louis II, at age nine, and his father
and sister Julie, landed in New York and then traveled on to Dakota and
came to the Elfrink farm near Bangor, S.D.

Louis' father worked on farms, doing general farm work and a few years
later, they took up a homestead southwest of Java. His father passed
away April 25, 1902 when Louis II was sixteen years old. Louis then
helped his mother farm for a number of years.

In 1912 Louis II, went to Canada where he farmed for 2 years. While
living there, he met and married Elizabeth Simmon on Jan. 4, 1914 at
Middle Lake, Sask., Canada. One son, Louis (Loy) III, was born to the
young couple before returning back to the Java area where they
homesteaded. Six more children were born to the Miklos family: Steve,
John Helen, Matilda, Art, and Daniel, all of whom are still living

Elizabeth passed away May 24, 1917 and Louis II is still residing on
the homestead with his son, Louis III, and his wife. His mother, Sophia
passed away Oct. 19, 1939 at Cleveland, Ohio.

Louis II did attend school in Hungary and also the West Spring Valley
school near Java, and many years later he was a member of the Spring
Valley School Board, where he served for three years. He still
reminisces of the times when he came to the United States and remembers
the severe blizzard of 1923.

                HEINRICH MUELLER FAMILY

Heinrich Mueller was born in Immenschlag, Post Gastern, Austria on July
3, 1885. He lived there with his parents until 1906 when he immigrated
to the United States He came as far as Waterville, Minn., where his
brother, Joseph lived and he worked there for awhile for $15.00 a
month. Not liking city living, he decided to come to his cousins' place
(The Stangl brothers), near Java, S.D. He worked for the Stangl
brothers for three years. He then homesteaded on a rocky quarter of
land west of Spring Lake

        Heinrich Mueller Homestead

He was a bachelor for two years, living in his claim shanty 14x16 feet.
He then met and married Martha Bollinger, who had immigrated to the
United States with her parents from Neudorf, Russia. They married on
May 24, 1911 at the farm home of Adolph Stangl. They lived in their
claim shanty and spent many hours working very hard. They purchased
their first milk cow and Heinrich had to take on an extra job with a
road crew to pay for it. They have many memories of the hard times they
had the first few years, such as their wheat running 6-7 bushels to the
acre and sold for 70 cents a bushel. Also the first year they planted
potatoes, the gophers dug them all up and ate them before they could
even start growing.

It was on this homestead that their nine children were born. Henry, the
oldest son was born on March 6, 1912 and he married Molly Nafts of
Huntley, Mont. They live on a farm southeast of Java and have one son
and two daughters. Otto was born on Oct. 11, 1913 and he married Anna
Hofmann of Worden, Mont. They live in Java, and have five daughters. On
Sept. 14, 1915, Anton was born and he married Evelyn Jackson of Ayden,
North Carolina. They live in La Crosse Wis. A fourth son, Carl, was
born on Aug. 11, 1917. He married Eunice Erbe and they live on a farm
near Britton, S.D. They have three sons and four daughters. The
Muellers first daughter, Emma, was born on Oct. 17, 1919. She married
Gay Raba and they have two sons and three daughters. They live on a
farm near New Underwood, S.D. On Feb. 7, 1922, another daughter Marie,
was born. She married Albert Schreter and they had one daughter. Marie
passed away during heart surgery in November, 1943. Her little daughter,
Charlotte, then made her home with her grandparents, the Heinrich
Muellers, living there until her marriage to Ervin Monthye Jr., of
Mound City, S.D. They now live in Sidney, Mont., and have three
children. Another son, Harold, joined the family on June 19, 1924. He
married Beverly Jones of Huron, and they have one daughter and two
sons. They live on a farm near Hitchcock, S. D. Their third daughter,
Anne, was born on May 21, 1926. She married John Witlock and they have
two sons and four daughters. Ervin joined the family on May 4, 1929. He
married Priscilla Biel, and they have one son and two daughters. They
still live on the family homestead southeast of Java.

        Mr. and Mrs. Heinrich Mueller
 
Mr. and Mrs. Mueller both worked very hard to make a living for the
large family. They had many happy and also sad memories of the days
gone by. Mr. Mueller was one of the men that helped organize the Java
Equity. He also served on the Board of Directors there for many years
as well as on the school board. They were life long members of the
Lutheran church in Java, and celebrated their 60th wedding anniversary
there in May, 1971. Mrs. Mueller passed away on May 3, 1973
at the age of 82 years. Mr. Mueller passed away on Aug. 18, 1974 at the
age of 89 years.

                GEORGE NAASZ I FAMILY

The family of George Naasz I, and Margaret (nee Miller), originated in
South Russia of German descent. Their parents migrated into Russia from
Germany in the early 1800's.

George, the father, died in Russia. The mother, Margaret accompanied
her children, all born in Russia, to America to seek new homes, thus
escaping the political and military rule in Russia. The oldest son,
Adam, remained in Russia, while his son, Adam Jr., accompanied the rest
of the families.

The family consists of Adam, William, George, Magdalena, Louise,
Christian, Ludwig, Fred, Andreas, and Margaretha. They arrived in the
United States at different times, some came over as late as 1907. Some
settled in Walworth county, some in Campbell county, and others
eventually settled in Kansas.

Adam, the oldest, remained in Russia. One son, Adam Jr., came to the
Dakota Territory. An adopted daughter, Helena Doebele, married John
Schlepp Jr., and they came to this country in 1889. No additional
information of the other children is known. Adam Jr., married a Stadel,
and their children were Gust, Otto, Bertha, and Adam III. His first
wife died and he married a Mindt, who had four children. They had one
child, Oscar.

William served in the Russian Army for five years and in 1880 was
married to Caroline Schlenker. Two daughters were born. Pauline
Eisenbeisz remained in Russia, and Christine married Jacob Olhauser,
came to the U.S. After the death of his first wife, he married Mrs.
Marie Weasz in 1896 and to this union six children were born, four of
which survive: Mrs. Wm. (Hulda) Oswald, Mrs. Martin (Freda)
Buckenberger, William and George. His second wife had two sons by a
first marriage, Reinhold and Edward.

George II married Magdalena Graf. They had eleven children, the oldest
daughter died in Russia, and two children died at the homestead. Eight
surviving children are as follows: Adolph married Margaret Miller and
when she died, he married Pauline Sackman; Magdalena married Christ
Schnaible; Ludwig married Louise Wahl; George married Lydia Gross;
Christina married Gottlieb Fiechtner; Christ married Tillie Sackman;
Emelia married John Fiechtner; and Edward married Martha Mayer.

Ludwig married Catherine Ehret. Their children were as follows: Mrs.
Jacob (Minnie) Merkel, Mrs. Ted (Louise) Kundert, Mrs. Edward (Pauline)
Miller, John, and Adam.

Magdalena married Johan Scherbenski, Their children were as follows:
George and Tobias, twins; John; William Mrs. Christ (Margaret) Grenz;
and Magdalena, deceased.

Louise married Karl Wahl. Their children were as follows: Fred; Mrs.
Alex (Amelia) Adolph; Mrs. Ellis (Rose) Hoffman; Karl; Ed; Mrs. Robert
(Lydia) Hepper; George; Albert; Mrs. John (Louise) Mutschler; Mrs. Larry
(Alvina) Ebert; and Julius, deceased.

Christian married. Their children were as follows: Gottlieb; John; and
Mrs. John (Pauline) Lange. After the death of his first wife, he
remarried. The children by the second marriage were as follows: Ludwig,
whose first wife was Katie Diede, and second wife was Caroline Diede
Schott; Christ married Emma Pfeiffe; Mrs. Henry (Martha) Diede,
Reinhold married Pauline Schlepp; George, Elizabeth Bertha, all
married; and Mrs. Jacob (Mary) Wolf.

Fred married Rosina Schuh. Their children were: Jacob, John, Fred, and
Margaret.

Andreas married. Their children were: Edward; Reinhold; Mrs. Helen
McCannis; Mrs. Walter (Mathilda) Winters; and John.

Margaretha married John Ehret. She died in childbirth.

        Mr. and Mrs. George Naasz II

                GEORGE NAASZ II FAMILY

George Naasz II was born in Hoffnungstal, South Russia on Sept. 8,
1855, son of George Naasz I and Margaret Miller Naasz. He was united in
marriage with Magdalena Graf on June 29, 1877. She was horn in
Hoffnungstahl, Rumania on May 18, 1855. George's brothers and sisters
were: Ludwig, Magdalena, Louise, Christian, Fred, Andreas, Margaretha,
Adam and William. Magdalena's brothers and sisters were as follows:
Katherine and Adolph that died in Rumania; and her half-brothers and
sisters were Adolph Hofer, John Hofer, Frederick Hofer, William Hofer,
and Carrie Hofer (Mrs. Martin Rieger); all immigrated to U.S.

The family immigrated to America in May, 1893, for the same reasons
that many other families did,--to escape the political and social
conditions, and to escape the military rule in Russia. The five older
children, Adolph, Magdalena, Ludwig, George, and Christina, came over
with their parents. Christ was born on the ship. Emelia and Edward were
born on the homestead in Walworth county, S.D. The oldest daughter
died in Russia, and two other children died on the homestead. Their
hardships and difficulties were similar to that of hundreds of other
immigrant farmers, and they met them with fortitude and courage.

They homesteaded on a farm five miles east of what is now Java. Their
first concern was of course to provide some sort of shelter. They built
a two-room sod house as a temporary dwelling in a corner of the yard,
and lived in this until the stone house was built. This stone house was
built out of stone, straw and mud. They built three rooms: one for the
folks, one for the children, and a kitchen, and then an additional room
for a summer kitchen. A barn was built onto the house, just one room
enough for a few horses and cows. Everything was built in a row. Later
there was a wooden barn built, and the other was torn down. When his
mother lived with them, they built an additional room for her to one
side of the house, but she ate with the family. A stone fence was built
to enclose the garden which had cherry, apple and other trees; rhubarb;
raspberries; etc.; vegetables; and hops for yeast to make bread. They
built a sod pig barn. A round smoke house was built also of mud and
onto the smoke house a round oven was built of mud. The oven was about
three feet high, and they would start a fire with straw, wood, cow
chips, etc. until it was real hot. Then they would take out all of the
ashes and place in the loaves of bread. Ten loaves of bread could be
baked at one time; even kuchen was baked this way. Everything would
bake nice and firm. They had a metal door on this and would seal it
with mud. The smoke house was enclosed with a metal door and had a pipe
out of the roof.

They drove to Hoffnungstal church about four miles away with a horse
and wagon, and in winter with a sled. Some of the families attending
the church were George Naaszs, Fred Naaszs, Baumans, Zweigardts,
Hindemiths, Schlepps, Horsts, Kapfensteins and Fiechtners. Before this
church was built, services were held in the homes, each taking their
turn. From the start, they were without a minister. The people that
lived in this community built the church.

They moved to Java in 1907. George, his brother Ludwig, and David
Blinderman bought a mercantile store and operated it in 1907 and 1908.
George sold his share to young Lou Helm. Then George and his son-in-law
Gottlieb Fiechtner bought the livery stable, and Gottlieb operated it
for about two years. The five older children were married, and Christ,
Emelia, and Edward moved to town with their parents.

They lived a quiet retired life, and were true and devoted Christians,
being members of the Lutheran faith all their lives. George passed away
March 28, 1924, and Magdalena died on Jan. 30, 1926.

Their children were as follows:

Adolph married Margaret Miller and had three children, Theodore,
Arthur, and Adolph Jr. After the death of Margaret, he married Pauline
Sackman, and their children were, Emil; Herman; Irene (Mrs. Arthur
Pfaff); Edgar; Reuben; Marvin; and Milton. Their two daughters, Alvina
and Esther died during the 1918 flu epidemic.

George III married Lydia Gross and their children were Ida
(Mrs.John Goetz); Alvin; Albert; Herbert; and Clifford.

Magadalena married Christ Schnaible, and their children were Berthold;
Otto; Arnold; Traugott; and a daughter, Laile, who died in 1919.

Ludwig married Louise Wahl, and their children were Martha, Edgar,
Edwin, Igan, and DeLila.

Christina married Gottlieb Fiechtner and their children were Emil,
Albert, Gerhardt and Erna (Mrs. Elmer Schlepp ) .

Christ married Tillie Sackman and their children were Othmer and Violet
(Mrs. Vernard Sieler).

Emelia married John Fiechtner and their children were Herbert; Irene
(Mrs. E. Levi Krause); and Gertrude (Mrs. Edgar Pietzke).

Edward married Martha Mayer. They had no children.

                GEORGE NAASZ III

George Naasz III was born in Hoffnungstal, South Russia on Oct. 15,
1889 and came to America with his parents in May 1893. The family
settled on a farm east of Java near the homestead of his father's
homestead.

        Mr. and Mrs. George Naasz III

On Nov. 13, 1906 George married Lydia Gross and bought a farm near the
homestead. They farmed and raised their family of four children. In
1935, when their children had left home they decided to retire so a
home was purchased and they moved to Java, where they led a retired
life.

The children were: Alvin (deceased) married Elsie Himmrich, had one son
and one daughter. Elsie now resides in Aberdeen, S.D.

Albert married Alvina Buechler and have 2 daughters and one son. They
now reside in Pierre, S.D.

Clifford married and has one son and resides at Herlong, Calif.

Ida married John Goetz, husband deceased. Have one daughter, Geraldine,
who lives on the homestead. Ida resides at the Good Samaritan Center at
Selby.

George resided in Java until his death on Dec. 25, 1958 at the age of
78 years. Lydia remained in Java for a few years and then later moved
to the Good Samaritan Center at Selby, S.D., until her death in July,
1969 at the age of 82 years.

        Mr. and Mrs. Edward Naasz

                EDWARD NAASZ

Edward Naasz, born Oct. 4, 1901 was the son of George Naasz Sr., who
immigrated to America and settled 4 miles east of Java. Edward grew to
young manhood while working as a farmhand for various farmers. He later
was employed at the Schlepp Drug until he married Martha Mayer, daughter
of Michael and Christina Mayer, on Aug. 14, 1924.

They farmed on the homestead of George Naasz for many years and later
moved on the Lakeman farm, but remained there only a short time and
then moved on the Spiry farm north of Java. A few years later they
moved on the Jake Helm farm and remained there until 1932 when they
moved to Java. He was employed by the County Highway Department, until
his death in January, 1972. His wife Martha was a cook for the Java
Public School for 12 years, and is now living a retired life in Java.

Many students were helped by Martha and Ed, by opening their home to
let them stay there for cheap board and room.

        Mr. and Mrs. Christ Naasz

                CHRIST NAASZ

Christ Naasz was the youngest son of George Naasz II and his wife,
Magdalena. He was born May 2, 1893 on board ship enroute to the United
States from Russia.

He grew to manhood on a farm east of Java, where his father and mother
and sisters and brothers had homesteaded. Years later, he went into
business owning and operating a Saloon in Java. Christ married Mathilda
(Tillie) Sackman on Dec . 26, 1918 in the Lutheran church in Java.

Christ sold his saloon and the young couple moved on his parental farm
east of Java. They farmed for eighteen years with the help from
Tillie's brother, Herman Sackman, who lived with them for many years.
The family moved to Java in 1938 where Christ was employed by Helms
Lumber Yard and later by Eisenbeisz Pool Hall. He became the manager of
the Java Municipal Liquor until his retirement.

Christ and Tillie were devout church members and assisted in janitor
services at the St. Paul Lutheran church for many years. Tillie was a
member of the church's Woman's Society and also a member of the church
choir until her death in 1967. Christ presently resides at the Good
Samaritan Center at Selby, S.D.

Two children were born to the Christ Naasz family who are:

Othmer ( Ole ) who served in the Army in World War II and married
Esther Job. They now reside in Mobridge, S.D., where they own and
operate Ole's T.V. They have two daughters, Vickie, married Eugene
Locatelli and living in Cheyenne, Wyo.; Gwynne, married Archie Bauman
and reside in Mobridge; one son David, attending Junior High in
Mobridge.

Violet married Vernard Sieler (a retired serviceman), is presently
employed as a Reading Specialist at Kent, Wash., was recognized as one
of the Outstanding Educators in America. They now reside in Renton,
Wash. They have one daughter, Wadena, married to Richard Spence and
have a daughter, Richelle and a son, Courtenay, and reside in Kent,
Wash. One son, Kevin, is attending Central Washington State College.

        Mr. and Mrs. John Naasz

                JOHN AND KATHRYN NAASZ

John Naasz was born in 1886 in Hoffnungstal, South Russia and came to
the United States with his parents at the age of 2 years.

John grew up to young manhood on his parents homestead 6-1/2 miles
northeast of Java. He worked hard helping his parents raise livestock
and farm grain. Little time was spent in school but religious services
were attended regularly.

In 1907, John married Kathryn Hofer, who also was born in South Russia
and came to the United States with her parents in 1903.

The young married couple bought a farm 3-1/2 miles from his father's
homestead. They remained on the farm until they retired. To this
couple, five children were born: Rose, married Emanuel Hofer and they
have one son and are living on a farm northeast of Java. Barnard,
married Alma Oster and have two daughters. They now reside in Java.
Gerhardt married Frances Bender and have two daughters. They now reside
in Java. Lorraine married Norman Fiedler and have two sons and three
daughters and they are living in Aberdeen, S.D. Calvin married
Darlene Wahl and they have three sons, and now reside in McLaughlin,
S.D.

In 1942, John and Kathryn retired from farming and purchased a home in
Java, where they resided until their deaths.

                FREDRICK AND ROSINA NAASZ

Fredrick and Rosina Naasz were both born in Hoffnungstal, South Russia.
This is where they grew up and were married.

On May 2, 1888, Mr. and Mrs. Fredrick Naasz and two children, John and
Fred, along with his mother, brother Andrew, and sister Margaret (Mrs.
John Ehret), Louise (Mrs. Karl Wahl) left Russia, to make a new home in
America.

After many long days on the ship, they arrived at New York and from
there they traveled by rail to the Dakota Territory. They arrived in
Eureka, S.D., and remained there long enough to buy food and other
provisions to aid them on their search for a homestead. They settled in
Walworth County and homesteaded six miles northeast of Java. Here they
built a sod house and started farming and also raising livestock.

Fredrick and Rosina raised a family of four children who were - John and
Fred, who came to America with their parents; and Margaret and Jacob,
who were born at the homestead.

In 1919 the Naasz family retired from farming and bought a home in
Java. Margaret moved to Java with her parents They resided in Java
until their death.

Margaret, the only living child is now making her home in the Good
Samaritan Center at Selby.

        Mr. and Mrs. Fredrick Naasz

                PETER L. NIES

Peter L. Nies was born in Wayne, Nebraska, Feb. 29, 1892 to Charles and
Louisa Nies. He came to South Dakota with his parents in 1908 and
resided on a farm south of Java. In 1911, he married Ethel E. Ryan.

They homesteaded near Dupree, returning to the Ryan farm in 1913. In
1915 they homesteaded near Elkalaka, Mont., later moving to Jones
county, south of Pierre. In 1923 they moved to a farm south of Java,
residing there until retiring to Java in 1957.

Peter and Ethel's family consisted of: Leo, deceased, who married
Lucille Artz of Bowdle. Allen is their son. Helen, graduated from
California University at Long Beach and is a supervisor in a California
school. Hazel married Ben Buckenberger of Java. They have three
children and live in Fresno and Pebble Beach, Calif. Winfred married
Helen Raybauld of Chicago. Their home is in Highland, Ind., and have
three children. Margaret married Rueben Boehler of Java. They have two
daughters, Jeanne and Kathy. Harold married Helen Witlock of Java. They
